## Q58

Chemistry · physical · Chemical Kinetics

The given graph is a representation of kinetics of a reaction.

The y and x axes for zero and first order reactions, respectively are

![printed figure: question](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/04112026/neet_papers/2022/2022_S1/figures_agent/q058_a7cb0eb8_04112026.png?v=a7cb0eb8e39d)

- **(1)** zero order ($y$ = rate and $x$ = concentration), first order ($y = t_{1/2}$ and $x$ = concentration)
- (2) zero order ($y$ = rate and $x$ = concentration), first order ($y$ = rate and $x = t_{1/2}$)
- (3) zero order ($y$ = concentration and $x$ = time), first order ($y = t_{1/2}$ and $x$ = concentration)
- (4) zero order ($y$ = concentration and $x$ = time), first order ($y$ = rate constant and $x$ = concentration)

**Answer** — booklet: (1) · independent solve: (1) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. For zero order reaction
2. $r = k[A]^{0}$
3. $r = k$ (constant)
4. hence, 'y' as 'rate' and 'x' as concentration will give desired graph.
5. For first order reaction
6. $t_{1/2} = \frac{0.693}{k}$ (constant)
7. hence, 'y' as '$t_{1/2}$' and 'x' as concentration will give desired graph.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. The graph is a horizontal line, so $y$ stays constant as $x$ changes.
2. Zero order: $\text{rate} = k[A]^0 = k$, so rate vs concentration is a horizontal line.
3. First order: $t_{1/2} = \frac{0.693}{k}$, independent of $[A]_0$, so $t_{1/2}$ vs concentration is a horizontal line.
4. Concentration vs time for zero order is a falling straight line, not horizontal, which rules out options $3$ and $4$; rate constant vs concentration is also constant but option $4$ fails on the zero-order part; option $2$ gives first-order rate vs $t_{1/2}$, which is not the standard plot. So the answer is option $1$.

**Subtopic:** Zero Order Reactions, First Order Reactions · **Difficulty:** medium (3 steps)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 23)

![printed question 58](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/04112026/neet_papers/2022/2022_S1/crops/q058_51f392af_04112026.png?v=51f392afad53)

## Q59

Chemistry · inorganic · Hydrogen

Match List-I with List-II.

Choose the correct answer from the options given below

| List-I | | List-II | |
|---|---|---|---|
| (a) | $\mathrm{MgH_2}$ | (i) | Electron precise |
| (b) | $\mathrm{GeH_4}$ | (ii) | Electron deficient |
| (c) | $\mathrm{B_2H_6}$ | (iii) | Electron rich |
| (d) | $\mathrm{HF}$ | (iv) | Ionic |

- (1) (a) – (i), (b) – (ii), (c) – (iv), (d) – (iii)
- (2) (a) – (ii), (b) – (iii), (c) – (iv), (d) – (i)
- **(3)** (a) – (iv), (b) – (i), (c) – (ii), (d) – (iii)
- (4) (a) – (iii), (b) – (i), (c) – (ii), (d) – (iv)

**Answer** — booklet: (3) · independent solve: (3) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. List – I (Hydrides)    List – II (Nature)
2. $\mathrm{MgH_2}$ — Ionic
3. $\mathrm{GeH_4}$ — Electron precise
4. $\mathrm{B_2H_6}$ — Electron deficient
5. $\mathrm{HF}$ — Electron rich
6. (a) – (iv), (b) – (i), (c) – (ii), (d) – (iii)

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. $\mathrm{MgH_2}$ is a saline (ionic) hydride of an s-block metal, so (a) – (iv).
2. $\mathrm{GeH_4}$ is a group 14 molecular hydride with exactly enough electrons for its bonds, so it is electron precise: (b) – (i).
3. $\mathrm{B_2H_6}$ is a group 13 hydride with too few electrons for normal two-centre bonds, so it is electron deficient: (c) – (ii).
4. $\mathrm{HF}$ has lone pairs on F beyond those used in bonding, so it is electron rich: (d) – (iii).
5. The match (a)–(iv), (b)–(i), (c)–(ii), (d)–(iii) is option $3$.

**Subtopic:** Hydrides · **Difficulty:** medium (3 steps)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 24)

![printed question 59](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/04112026/neet_papers/2022/2022_S1/crops/q059_78b5cb7d_04112026.png?v=78b5cb7d3f1b)

## Q60

Chemistry · inorganic · The p–Block Elements

Given below are two statements
In the light of the above statements, choose the most appropriate answer from the options given below :

- (Statement I) The boiling points of the following hydrides of group 16 elements increases in the order – $\mathrm{H_2O} < \mathrm{H_2S} < \mathrm{H_2Se} < \mathrm{H_2Te}$
- (Statement II) The boiling points of these hydrides increase with increase in molar mass.

- (1) Statement I is correct but Statement II is incorrect
- (2) Statement I is incorrect but Statement II is correct
- (3) Both Statement I and Statement II are correct
- **(4)** Both Statement I and Statement II are incorrect

**Answer** — booklet: (4) · independent solve: (4) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Compound — Boiling point (K)
2. $\mathrm{H_2O}$ — $373$
3. $\mathrm{H_2S}$ — $213$
4. $\mathrm{H_2Se}$ — $232$
5. $\mathrm{H_2Te}$ — $269$
6. The boiling points of these hybrids not exactly increases with increase in molar mass.
7. $\mathrm{H_2O}$ has maximum boiling point due to intermolecular hydrogen bonding.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Among group 16 hydrides, $\mathrm{H_2O}$ forms extensive intermolecular hydrogen bonds, giving it the highest boiling point (373 K).
2. Actual order: $\mathrm{H_2S} < \mathrm{H_2Se} < \mathrm{H_2Te} < \mathrm{H_2O}$, so Statement I ($\mathrm{H_2O} < \mathrm{H_2S} < \ldots$) is incorrect.
3. $\mathrm{H_2O}$ has the lowest molar mass yet the highest boiling point, so the boiling points of these hydrides do not increase with molar mass; Statement II is incorrect.
4. Both statements incorrect, which is option $4$.

**Subtopic:** Important Compounds & their Hydrides & Oxides Group 16 · **Difficulty:** hard (2 steps)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 24)

## Q61

Chemistry · organic · Haloalkanes and Haloarenes

Which of the following sequence of reactions is suitable to synthesize chlorobenzene?

- (1) ![printed figure: option_1](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/04112026/neet_papers/2022/2022_S1/figures_agent/q061_option_1_beb9220e_04112026.png?v=beb9220e49df) — $\mathrm{HCl}$
- (2) ![printed figure: option_2](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/04112026/neet_papers/2022/2022_S1/figures_agent/q061_option_2_f3082291_04112026.png?v=f3082291c405) — $\mathrm{HCl}$, Heating
- **(3)** Benzene, $\mathrm{Cl_2}$, anhydrous $\mathrm{FeCl_3}$
- (4) Phenol, $\mathrm{NaNO_2}$, $\mathrm{HCl}$, $\mathrm{CuCl}$

**Answer** — booklet: (3) · independent solve: (3) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Benzene reacts with chlorine in presence of anhydrous $\mathrm{FeCl_3}$ to give chlorobenzene

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Chlorobenzene synthesis via electrophilic aromatic substitution requires benzene + Cl2 with a Lewis acid catalyst like anhydrous FeCl3.
2. This generates the electrophile $\mathrm{Cl^+}$ which substitutes a hydrogen on the benzene ring to give chlorobenzene directly.
3. Option 4 is incorrect because diazotization (NaNO2/HCl) followed by CuCl requires an aromatic amine (aniline), not phenol; phenol does not undergo this sequence to give chlorobenzene.
4. Options 1 and 2 involve unspecified structures reacting with HCl (with/without heating), which do not correspond to a standard chlorobenzene synthesis route taught in NCERT.
5. Hence, option 3 (Benzene, Cl2, anhydrous FeCl3) is the correct and standard method.

**Subtopic:** Preparation of Haloarenes · **Difficulty:** medium (2 steps)

## Q62

Chemistry · inorganic · The p–Block Elements

Given below are two statements: one is labelled as **Assertion (A)** and the other is labelled as **Reason (R)**.
In the light of the above statements, choose the most **appropriate** answer from the options given below:

- Assertion (A) $\mathrm{ICl}$ is more reactive than $\mathrm{I_2}$.
- Reason (R) $\mathrm{I}$-$\mathrm{Cl}$ bond is weaker than $\mathrm{I}$-$\mathrm{I}$ bond.

- (1) (A) is correct but (R) is not correct
- (2) (A) is not correct but (R) is correct
- **(3)** Both (A) and (R) are correct and (R) is the correct explanation of (A).
- (4) Both (A) and (R) are correct but (R) is not the correct explanation of (A).

**Answer** — booklet: (3) · independent solve: (3) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. In general, interhalogen compounds are more reactive than halogens (except fluorine).
2. This is because $\mathrm{X-X'}$ bond in interhalogens is weaker than $\mathrm{X-X}$ bond in halogens excepts $\mathrm{F-F}$ bond.
3. Therefore $\mathrm{I-Cl}$ is more reactive than $\mathrm{I_2}$ because of weaker $\mathrm{I-Cl}$ bond then $\mathrm{I-I}$ bond.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. NCERT teaches that interhalogen compounds are generally more reactive than the parent halogens (except $\mathrm{F_2}$), so $\mathrm{ICl}$ is more reactive than $\mathrm{I_2}$: (A) is correct.
2. NCERT gives the reason as the $\mathrm{X{-}X'}$ bond in interhalogens being weaker than the $\mathrm{X{-}X}$ bond in halogens, so (R) is correct and explains (A), which is option $3$.

**Subtopic:** Interhalogen Compounds & Important Compounds · **Difficulty:** hard (3 steps)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 25)

## Q63

Chemistry · inorganic · The D- and F-Block Elements – Practice

Gadolinium has a low value of third ionisation enthalpy because of

- (1) high electronegativity
- (2) high basic character
- (3) small size
- **(4)** high exchange enthalpy

**Answer** — booklet: (4) · independent solve: (4) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Electronic configuration of Gadolinium
2. $\mathrm{Gd}$ :— $[\mathrm{Xe}]\,4f^{7}\,5d^{1}\,6s^{2}$
3. In case of $3^{\mathrm{rd}}$ ionisation enthalpy electron will be removed from $5d$ and resultant configuration will be $[\mathrm{Xe}]4f^{7}$ that is stable electronic configuration as it will have high exchange energy, hence less energy will be required to remove $3^{\mathrm{rd}}$ electron.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Gd ($Z=64$) has configuration $[\mathrm{Xe}]\,4f^7 5d^1 6s^2$.
2. After two ionisations, $\mathrm{Gd^{2+}} = [\mathrm{Xe}]\,4f^7 5d^1$.
3. The third ionisation removes the $5d^1$ electron, giving $\mathrm{Gd^{3+}} = [\mathrm{Xe}]\,4f^7$, a half-filled $4f$ subshell.
4. The half-filled $4f^7$ state is stabilised by high exchange enthalpy, so $\Delta_i H_3$ is low, which is option $4$.

**Subtopic:** Ionisation Enthalpies, Formation of Coloured Ions & Formation of Complex Compounds · **Difficulty:** medium (2 steps)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 25)

## Q64

Chemistry · physical · Electrochemistry

At 298 K, the standard electrode potentials of $\mathrm{Cu^{2+}}$ / $\mathrm{Cu}$, $\mathrm{Zn^{2+}}$ / $\mathrm{Zn}$, $\mathrm{Fe^{2+}}$ / $\mathrm{Fe}$ and $\mathrm{Ag^{+}}$ / $\mathrm{Ag}$ are $0.34\,\mathrm{V}$, $-0.76\,\mathrm{V}$, $-0.44\,\mathrm{V}$ and $0.80\,\mathrm{V}$, respectively.
On the basis of standard electrode potential, predict which of the following reaction cannot occur?

- (1) $\mathrm{FeSO_4(aq)} + \mathrm{Zn(s)} \rightarrow \mathrm{ZnSO_4(aq)} + \mathrm{Fe(s)}$
- **(2)** $2\mathrm{CuSO_4(aq)} + 2\mathrm{Ag(s)} \rightarrow 2\mathrm{Cu(s)} + \mathrm{Ag_2SO_4(aq)}$
- (3) $\mathrm{CuSO_4(aq)} + \mathrm{Zn(s)} \rightarrow \mathrm{ZnSO_4(aq)} + \mathrm{Cu(s)}$
- (4) $\mathrm{CuSO_4(aq)} + \mathrm{Fe(s)} \rightarrow \mathrm{FeSO_4(aq)} + \mathrm{Cu(s)}$

**Answer** — booklet: (2) · independent solve: (2) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. For a reaction to be spontaneous, $\mathrm{E^{o}_{cell}}$ must be positive.
2. For, $\mathrm{FeSO_4(aq)} + \mathrm{Zn(s)} \rightarrow \mathrm{ZnSO_4(aq)} + \mathrm{Fe(s)}$
3. $\mathrm{E^{o}_{cell}} = \mathrm{E^{o}_{cathode}} - \mathrm{E^{o}_{anode}}$
4. $= -0.44\,\mathrm{V} - (-0.76\,\mathrm{V})$
5. $= 0.32\,\mathrm{V}$
6. For, $2\mathrm{CuSO_4(aq)} + 2\mathrm{Ag(s)} \rightarrow 2\mathrm{Cu(s)} + \mathrm{Ag_2SO_4(aq)}$
7. $\mathrm{E^{o}_{cell}} = 0.34\,\mathrm{V} - 0.80\,\mathrm{V}$
8. $= -0.46\,\mathrm{V}$
9. For, $\mathrm{CuSO_4(aq)} + \mathrm{Zn(s)} \rightarrow \mathrm{ZnSO_4(aq)} + \mathrm{Cu(s)}$
10. $\mathrm{E^{o}_{cell}} = 0.34\,\mathrm{V} - (-0.76\,\mathrm{V})$
11. $= 1.1\,\mathrm{V}$
12. For, $\mathrm{CuSO_4(aq)} + \mathrm{Fe(s)} \rightarrow \mathrm{FeSO_4(aq)} + \mathrm{Cu(s)}$
13. $\mathrm{E^{o}_{cell}} = 0.80\,\mathrm{V} - (-0.44\,\mathrm{V})$
14. $= 1.24\,\mathrm{V}$

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. A displacement reaction occurs if $E^\circ_{cell} = E^\circ_{cathode} - E^\circ_{anode} > 0$.
2. Option 3: $E^\circ_{cell} = 0.34 - (-0.76) = +1.10\,\mathrm{V}$, feasible.
3. Option 4: $E^\circ_{cell} = 0.34 - (-0.44) = +0.78\,\mathrm{V}$, feasible.
4. Option 1: $E^\circ_{cell} = -0.44 - (-0.76) = +0.32\,\mathrm{V}$, feasible.
5. Option 2: $E^\circ_{cell} = 0.34 - 0.80 = -0.46\,\mathrm{V} < 0$, so it cannot occur; answer is option $2$.

**Subtopic:** Electrochemical Series · **Difficulty:** easy (2 steps)

## Q65

Chemistry · inorganic · The s-Block Elements

Identify the **incorrect** statement from the following.

- (1) In an atom, all the five $3d$ orbitals are equal in energy in free state.
- **(2)** The shapes of $d_{xy}$, $d_{yz}$ and $d_{zx}$ orbitals are similar to each other; and $d_{x^2-y^2}$ and $d_{z^2}$ are similar to each other.
- (3) All the five $5d$ orbitals are different in size when compared to the respective $4d$ orbitals.
- (4) All the five $4d$ orbitals have shapes similar to the respective $3d$ orbitals.

**Answer** — booklet: (2) · independent solve: (2) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. In an atom, all the five $3d$ orbitals are equal in energy in free state $i.e.$, degenerate.
2. The shape of $d_{x^2-y^2}$ is different then shape of $d_{z^2}$
3. The size of orbital depends on principal quantum number 'n' therefore all the five $3d$ orbitals are different in size when compared to the respective $4d$ orbitals.
4. Shape of orbitals depends on azimuthal quantum number 'l' therefore shapes of $4d$ orbitals are similar to the respective $3d$ orbitals.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Option 1: In a free atom (no external field), all five 3d orbitals are degenerate (equal energy) - this is correct.
2. Option 2: $d_{xy}, d_{yz}, d_{zx}$ share the same four-lobed shape oriented between axes - true for these three. But $d_{x^2-y^2}$ (four lobes along axes) and $d_{z^2}$ (two lobes with a torus) have fundamentally different shapes, not similar to each other - so this statement is incorrect.
3. Option 3: Size of orbitals increases with principal quantum number $n$, so 5d orbitals are indeed larger than corresponding 4d orbitals - correct.
4. Option 4: Orbital shape depends only on azimuthal quantum number $l$, not $n$, so 4d and 3d orbitals have identical shapes - correct.
5. Hence the incorrect statement is option 2.

**Subtopic:** Alkali Metals: Properties and Trends · **Difficulty:** hard (4 steps)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 26)

## Q66

Chemistry · inorganic · Coordination Compounds – Practice

The IUPAC name of the complex- $\mathrm{[Ag(H_2O)_2][Ag(CN)_2]}$ is:

- (1) dicyanidosilver(I) diaquaargentate(I)
- **(2)** diaquasilver(I) dicyanidoargentate(I)
- (3) dicyanidosilver(II) diaquaargentate(II)
- (4) diaquasilver(II) dicyanidoargentate(II)

**Answer** — booklet: (2) · independent solve: (2) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. $\mathrm{[Ag(H_2O)_2][Ag(CN)_2]}$
2. IUPAC name : diaquasilver(I)dicyanidoargentate(I)

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. The salt splits into the cation $[\mathrm{Ag(H_2O)_2}]^{+}$ and the anion $[\mathrm{Ag(CN)_2}]^{-}$; the cation is named first.
2. Cation: $x + 2(0) = +1 \Rightarrow x = +1$, so it is diaquasilver(I).
3. Anion: $x + 2(-1) = -1 \Rightarrow x = +1$, and an anionic complex of Ag takes the name argentate, so it is dicyanidoargentate(I).
4. Full name: diaquasilver(I) dicyanidoargentate(I), which is option $2$.

**Subtopic:** IUPAC Nomenclature and Formulae of Coordination Compounds · **Difficulty:** medium (3 steps)

## Q67

Chemistry · physical · Electrochemistry

Given below are half cell reactions:
$\mathrm{MnO_4^-} + 8\mathrm{H^+} + 5\mathrm{e^-} \rightarrow \mathrm{Mn^{2+}} + 4\mathrm{H_2O}$
$E^{\circ}_{\mathrm{Mn^{2+}/MnO_4^-}} = -1.510\,\mathrm{V}$
$\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{O_2} + 2\mathrm{H^+} + 2\mathrm{e^-} \rightarrow \mathrm{H_2O}$
$E^{\circ}_{\mathrm{O_2/H_2O}} = +1.223\,\mathrm{V}$
Will the permanganate ion, $\mathrm{MnO_4^-}$ liberate $\mathrm{O_2}$ from water in the presence of an acid?

- (1) Yes, because $E^{\circ}_{\mathrm{cell}} = +\,2.733\,\mathrm{V}$
- (2) No, because $E^{\circ}_{\mathrm{cell}} = -\,2.733\,\mathrm{V}$
- **(3)** Yes, because $E^{\circ}_{\mathrm{cell}} = +\,0.287\,\mathrm{V}$
- (4) No, because $E^{\circ}_{\mathrm{cell}} = -\,0.287\,\mathrm{V}$

**Answer** — booklet: (3) · independent solve: (3) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. $\mathrm{MnO_4^-} + 8\mathrm{H^+} + 5\mathrm{e^-} \longrightarrow \mathrm{Mn^{2+}} + 4\mathrm{H_2O}$ \quad \dots(i)
2. $E^{\circ}_{\mathrm{MnO_4^-/Mn^{2+}}} = -\,E^{\circ}_{\mathrm{Mn^{2+}/MnO_4^-}} = 1.51\,\mathrm{V}$
3. $\mathrm{H_2O} \longrightarrow \frac{1}{2}\mathrm{O_2} + 2\mathrm{H^+} + 2\mathrm{e^-}$ \quad \dots(ii)
4. $E^{\circ}_{\mathrm{O_2/H_2O}} = 1.223\,\mathrm{V}$
5. Using $2 \times$ (i) $+\ 5 \times$ (ii), net cell reactions is
6. $2\mathrm{MnO_4^-} + 6\mathrm{H^+} \longrightarrow 2\mathrm{Mn^{2+}} + \frac{5}{2}\mathrm{O_2} + 3\mathrm{H_2O}$
7. $E^{\circ}_{\mathrm{cell}} = E^{\circ}_{\mathrm{C}} - E^{\circ}_{\mathrm{A}} = E^{\circ}_{\mathrm{MnO_4^-/Mn^{2+}}} - E^{\circ}_{\mathrm{O_2/H_2O}} = 1.51 - 1.223 = 0.287\,\mathrm{V}$
8. Since $E^{\circ}_{\mathrm{cell}} > 0$, therefore net cell reaction is spontaneous and so $\mathrm{MnO_4^-}$ liberate $\mathrm{O_2}$ from $\mathrm{H_2O}$ in presence of an acid.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. The given value -1.510 V is for the oxidation half reaction Mn2+ → MnO4-; hence reduction potential for MnO4- + 8H+ +5e- → Mn2+ +4H2O is +1.510 V.
2. For O2 + 4H+ +4e- → 2H2O, E° = +1.223 V (reduction); water oxidized to O2 at anode.
3. E°cell = E°(reduction, MnO4-/Mn2+) − E°(reduction, O2/H2O) = 1.510 − 1.223 = +0.287 V.
4. Positive E°cell means MnO4- can indeed oxidize water to liberate O2 in acidic medium.

**Subtopic:** Cell Potential · **Difficulty:** hard (3 steps)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 27)

## Q68

Chemistry · physical · Basic Concepts of Chemistry

What mass of 95% pure $\mathrm{CaCO_3}$ will be required to neutralise $50\,\mathrm{mL}$ of $0.5\,\mathrm{M}$ $\mathrm{HCl}$ solution according to the following reaction?
$\mathrm{CaCO_{3(s)} + 2HCl_{(aq)} \rightarrow CaCl_{2(aq)} + CO_{2(g)} + 2H_2O_{(l)}}$
[Calculate upto second place of decimal point]

- (1) $3.65\,\mathrm{g}$
- (2) $9.50\,\mathrm{g}$
- (3) $1.25\,\mathrm{g}$
- **(4)** $1.32\,\mathrm{g}$

**Answer** — booklet: (4) · independent solve: (4) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Let m gram mass of $\mathrm{CaCO_3}$ is required
2. Pure $\mathrm{CaCO_3}$ in m gram $= \frac{95}{100} \times \mathrm{m}$
3. Moles of $\mathrm{CaCO_3} = \frac{95}{100} \times \frac{\mathrm{m}}{100}$
4. Moles of $\mathrm{HCl}$ required $= 2 \times$ moles of $\mathrm{CaCO_3}$
5. $= 2 \times \frac{95}{100} \times \frac{\mathrm{m}}{100}$
6. $2 \times \frac{95}{100} \times \frac{\mathrm{m}}{100} = \frac{50}{1000} \times 0.5$
7. $\mathrm{m} = 1.315\,\mathrm{g} \approx 1.32\,\mathrm{g}$

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. $n_{\mathrm{HCl}} = 0.050\,\mathrm{L} \times 0.5\,\mathrm{M} = 0.025\,\mathrm{mol}$.
2. From the equation, $1\,\mathrm{mol\ CaCO_3}$ reacts with $2\,\mathrm{mol\ HCl}$, so $n_{\mathrm{CaCO_3}} = \frac{0.025}{2} = 0.0125\,\mathrm{mol}$.
3. Mass of pure $\mathrm{CaCO_3} = 0.0125 \times 100\,\mathrm{g\,mol^{-1}} = 1.25\,\mathrm{g}$.
4. The sample is $95\%$ pure, so required mass $= \frac{1.25}{0.95} = 1.3158\,\mathrm{g} \approx 1.32\,\mathrm{g}$, which is option $4$.

**Subtopic:** Stoichiometry · **Difficulty:** hard (4 steps)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 28)

![printed question 68](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/04112026/neet_papers/2022/2022_S1/crops/q068_9b03d295_04112026.png?v=9b03d295cb73)

## Q69

Chemistry · organic · Isomerism in Organic Compounds

The **incorrect** statement regarding chirality is

- **(1)** Enantiomers are superimposable mirror images on each other
- (2) A racemic mixture shows zero optical rotation
- (3) $\mathrm{S_N}1$ reaction yields $1 : 1$ mixture of both enantiomers
- (4) The product obtained by $\mathrm{S_N}2$ reaction of haloalkane having chirality at the reactive site shows inversion of configuration

**Answer** — booklet: (1) · independent solve: (1) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. The stereoisomers related to each other as non-superimposable mirror image are called enantiomers.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Enantiomers are, by definition, non-superimposable mirror images of each other, so option $1$ is incorrect.
2. Options $3$ (SN1 gives racemisation, $1:1$ mixture as NCERT teaches), $2$ (SN2 backside attack gives inversion) and $4$ (racemic mixture has zero net rotation) are correct, so the answer is option $1$.

**Subtopic:** Optical Isomerism · **Difficulty:** easy (1 step)

## Q70

Chemistry · physical · Solutions

In one molal solution that contains $0.5$ mole of a solute, there is

- (1) $100\,\mathrm{mL}$ of solvent
- (2) $1000\,\mathrm{g}$ of solvent
- (3) $500\,\mathrm{mL}$ of solvent
- **(4)** $500\,\mathrm{g}$ of solvent

**Answer** — booklet: (4) · independent solve: (4) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Molality is the moles of solute dissolved per kg of solvent therefore $500\,\mathrm{g}$, $1$ molal solution contains $0.5$ of solute, as
2. $\mathrm{m} = \frac{\text{Moles of solute}}{\text{Mass of solvent (in kg)}}$
3. $1 = \frac{0.5}{\text{Mass of solvent (in kg)}}$
4. $\therefore$ Mass of solvent (in kg) $= 0.5$
5. $= 500\,\mathrm{g}$

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Molality is defined as $m = \frac{n_{\text{solute}}}{W_{\text{solvent}}\,(\mathrm{kg})}$.
2. Rearranging, $W_{\text{solvent}} = \frac{n_{\text{solute}}}{m} = \frac{0.5\,\mathrm{mol}}{1\,\mathrm{mol\,kg^{-1}}} = 0.5\,\mathrm{kg}$.
3. $0.5\,\mathrm{kg} = 500\,\mathrm{g}$ of solvent, which is option $4$.

**Subtopic:** Molality · **Difficulty:** medium (1 step)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 28)

![printed question 70](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/04112026/neet_papers/2022/2022_S1/crops/q070_fd00bca0_04112026.png?v=fd00bca07c83)

![printed solution 70](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/04112026/neet_papers/2022/2022_S1/solutions/q070_00b20d79_04112026.png?v=00b20d791282)

## Q71

Chemistry · inorganic · Chemical Bonding and Molecular Structure

Which amongst the following is **incorrect** statement?

- (1) $\mathrm{H_2^+}$ ion has one electron
- **(2)** $\mathrm{O_2^+}$ ion is diamagnetic
- (3) The bond orders of $\mathrm{O_2^+}, \mathrm{O_2}, \mathrm{O_2^-}$ and $\mathrm{O_2^{2-}}$ are 2.5, 2, 1.5 and 1, respectively
- (4) $\mathrm{C_2}$ molecule has four electrons in its two degenerate $\pi$ molecular orbitals

**Answer** — booklet: (2) · independent solve: (2) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. $\sigma 1s^{2}\sigma^{*}1s^{2}\sigma 2s^{2}\sigma^{*}2s^{2}\sigma 2p_{z}^{2}\quad \begin{matrix}\pi 2p_{x}^{2}\\ \pi 2p_{y}^{2}\end{matrix}\quad \begin{matrix}\pi^{*}2p_{x}^{1}\\ \pi 2p_{y}\end{matrix}$
2. Due to one unpaired electron in $\pi^{*}2p$ molecular orbital, $\mathrm{O_2^{+}}$ is a paramagnetic ion.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Option 3: bond order $= \frac{N_b - N_a}{2}$; $\mathrm{O_2^{+}} = \frac{10-5}{2} = 2.5$, $\mathrm{O_2} = \frac{10-6}{2} = 2$, $\mathrm{O_2^{-}} = \frac{10-7}{2} = 1.5$, $\mathrm{O_2^{2-}} = \frac{10-8}{2} = 1$, so the statement is correct.
2. Option 4: $\mathrm{C_2}$ (12 electrons) has configuration $\sigma 1s^2 \sigma^* 1s^2 \sigma 2s^2 \sigma^* 2s^2 (\pi 2p_x^2 = \pi 2p_y^2)$, i.e. four electrons in the two degenerate $\pi$ MOs, so the statement is correct.
3. Option 1: $\mathrm{H_2^{+}}$ has $1 + 1 - 1 = 1$ electron, so the statement is correct.
4. Option 2: $\mathrm{O_2^{+}}$ has 15 electrons, leaving one unpaired electron in $\pi^* 2p$, so it is paramagnetic; calling it diamagnetic is incorrect.
5. The incorrect statement is option $2$.

**Subtopic:** Molecular Orbital Theory · **Difficulty:** medium (1 step)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 29)

## Q72

Chemistry · organic · Aldehydes, Ketones, and Carboxylic Acids

Match List-I with List-II.

List – I (Products formed)

List – II (Reaction of carbonyl compound with)

Choose the correct answer from the options given below

| List-I | | List-II | |
|---|---|---|---|
| (a) | Cyanohydrin | (i) | $\mathrm{NH_2OH}$ |
| (b) | Acetal | (ii) | $\mathrm{RNH_2}$ |
| (c) | Schiff's base | (iii) | alcohol |
| (d) | Oxime | (iv) | $\mathrm{HCN}$ |

- (1) (a) – (i), (b) – (iii), (c) – (ii), (d) – (iv)
- **(2)** (a) – (iv), (b) – (iii), (c) – (ii), (d) – (i)
- (3) (a) – (iii), (b) – (iv), (c) – (ii), (d) – (i)
- (4) (a) – (ii), (b) – (iii), (c) – (iv), (d) – (i)

**Answer** — booklet: (2) · independent solve: (2) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. List – I (Products formed) : Cyanohydrin — List – II (Reaction of carbonyl compound with) : $\mathrm{HCN}$
2. Acetal — Alcohol
3. Schiff's base — $\mathrm{RNH_2}$
4. Oxime — $\mathrm{NH_2OH}$
5. (a) – (iv), (b) – (iii), (c) – (ii), (d) – (i)

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Carbonyl $+ \mathrm{HCN}$ gives cyanohydrin, so (a)–(iv).
2. Carbonyl $+$ alcohol (dry HCl) gives acetal, so (b)–(iii).
3. Carbonyl $+ \mathrm{RNH_2}$ gives a Schiff's base (imine), so (c)–(ii).
4. Carbonyl $+ \mathrm{NH_2OH}$ gives oxime, so (d)–(i).
5. The set (a)–(iv), (b)–(iii), (c)–(ii), (d)–(i) is option $2$.

**Subtopic:** Nucleophilic Addition Reactions · **Difficulty:** easy (1 step)

## Q73

Chemistry · inorganic · The p–Block Elements

Choose the correct statement:

- **(1)** Diamond is $\mathrm{sp^3}$ hybridised and graphite is $\mathrm{sp^2}$ hybridized.
- (2) Both diamond and graphite are used as dry lubricants.
- (3) Diamond and graphite have two dimensional network.
- (4) Diamond is covalent and graphite is ionic.

**Answer** — booklet: (1) · independent solve: (1) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Diamond : $\bullet$ $sp^{3}$ hybridised carbon atom
2. $\bullet$ Covalent solid
3. $\bullet$ 3-D structure
4. $\bullet$ Cannot be used as dry lubricant
5. Graphite : $\bullet$ $sp^{2}$ hybridised carbon atom
6. $\bullet$ Covalent solid
7. $\bullet$ 3-D structure
8. $\bullet$ Used as dry lubricant

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Diamond: each C is tetrahedrally bonded to four C atoms, so it is $\mathrm{sp^3}$ with a three-dimensional network; option $3$ is false.
2. Graphite: each C is bonded to three C atoms in hexagonal layers, so it is $\mathrm{sp^2}$; both solids are covalent, so option $4$ is false.
3. Only graphite (slippery layers) is a dry lubricant, so option $2$ is false.
4. Diamond $\mathrm{sp^3}$ and graphite $\mathrm{sp^2}$ is correct: option $1$.

**Subtopic:** Catenation & Allotropy Group 14 · **Difficulty:** easy (1 step)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 30)

## Q74

Chemistry · organic · Aldehydes, Ketones, and Carboxylic Acids

Given below are two statements :
In the light of the above statements, choose the **most appropriate** answer from the given below

- (Statement I) The boiling points of aldehydes and ketones are higher than hydrocarbons of comparable molecular masses because of weak molecular association in aldehydes and ketones due to dipole - dipole interactions.
- (Statement II) The boiling points of aldehydes and ketones are lower than the alcohols of similar molecular masses due to the absence of H-bonding.

- (1) **Statement I** is correct but **Statement II** is incorrect
- (2) **Statement I** is incorrect but **Statement II** is correct
- **(3)** Both **Statement I** and **Statement II** are correct
- (4) Both **Statement I** and **Statement II** are incorrect

**Answer** — booklet: (3) · independent solve: (3) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. The boiling points of aldehydes and ketones are higher than hydrocarbons of comparable molecular masses due to weak molecular association in aldehydes and ketones arising out of the dipole - dipole interaction.
2. Alcohols involved intermolecular hydrogen bonding, because of which the boiling point of aldehydes and ketones are lower than the alcohols of similar molecular masses.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Statement I: the polar $C=O$ group gives weak intermolecular association via dipole-dipole interactions, so aldehydes and ketones boil higher than hydrocarbons of comparable molecular mass; Statement I is correct.
2. Statement II: aldehydes and ketones lack $O-H$ and cannot form intermolecular H-bonds, so they boil lower than alcohols of similar molecular mass; Statement II is correct.
3. Both statements are correct, which is option $3$.

**Subtopic:** Physical Properties of Aldehydes, Ketones, and Carboxylic Acids · **Difficulty:** medium (3 steps)

- (2) ![printed figure: option_2](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/04112026/neet_papers/2022/2022_S1/figures_agent/q075_option_2_b85def2c_04112026.png?v=b85def2c3fe1)
- (3) ![printed figure: option_3](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/04112026/neet_papers/2022/2022_S1/figures_agent/q075_option_3_5a020e39_04112026.png?v=5a020e39c380)
- **(4)** ![printed figure: option_4](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/04112026/neet_papers/2022/2022_S1/figures_agent/q075_option_4_994caacf_04112026.png?v=994caacfec62)

**Answer** — booklet: (4) · independent solve: (4) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Work done under any thermodynamic process can be determined by area under the ‘p-V’ graph.
2. As it can be observed maximum area is covered in option ‘4’.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Work done equals the area under the $p$-$V$ curve, $W = \int p\,dV$.
2. Option $1$ is a vertical line ($\Delta V = 0$), so $W = 0$.
3. Option $3$ is a small closed cycle; its work is only the small enclosed loop area.
4. Options $2$ and $4$ are similar isothermal expansions, but option $4$ extends over a much larger $\Delta V$ and has the larger area under it.
5. So the maximum work is option $4$.

**Subtopic:** Work Done · **Difficulty:** medium (2 steps)

## Q76

Chemistry · organic · Alcohols, Phenols and Ethers

Given below are two statements
In the light of the above statements, choose the **most appropriate** answer from the options given below:

- (Statement I) The acidic strength of monosubstituted nitrophenol is higher than phenol because of electron withdrawing nitro group.
- (Statement II) $o$-nitrophenol, $m$-nitrophenol and $p$-nitrophenol will have same acidic strength as they have one nitro group attached to the phenolic ring.

- **(1)** **Statement I** is correct but **Statement II** is incorrect.
- (2) **Statement I** is incorrect but **Statement II** is correct.
- (3) Both **Statement I** and **Statement II** are correct.
- (4) Both **Statement I** and **Statement II** are incorrect.

**Answer** — booklet: (1) · independent solve: (1) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Nitro group has electron withdrawing tendency. It can withdraw electrons both by $-\mathrm{I}$ effect and $-\mathrm{R}$ effect. Thus the acidic strength of monosubstituted nitrophenol is higher than phenol.
2. Nitro group present at $o$- and $p$-positions will have strong $-\mathrm{R}$ effect while nitro group present at $m$-position will influence only $-\mathrm{I}$ effect hence acidity or o/p isomer will be more meta isomer.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Nitro group is electron-withdrawing, stabilizing the phenoxide ion via resonance and induction, so nitrophenol is more acidic than phenol — Statement I is correct.
2. Position of NO2 affects acid strength: ortho and para nitrophenol show stronger resonance stabilization of the conjugate base than meta, so the three isomers do NOT have the same acidic strength — Statement II is incorrect.
3. Hence Statement I correct, Statement II incorrect, matching option 1.

**Subtopic:** Acidity of Phenols · **Difficulty:** medium (2 steps)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 31)

![printed question 76](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/04112026/neet_papers/2022/2022_S1/crops/q076_1d59ee90_04112026.png?v=1d59ee90394e)

Publisher's solution, as printed:

![printed solution 76](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/04112026/neet_papers/2022/2022_S1/solutions/q076_f5c4484c_04112026.png?v=f5c4484cc639)

## Q77

Chemistry · physical · Ionic Equilibrium

The pH of the solution containing 50 mL each of $0.10\,\mathrm{M}$ sodium acetate and $0.01\,\mathrm{M}$ acetic acid is [Given $\mathrm{p}K_\mathrm{a}$ of $\mathrm{CH_3COOH} = 4.57$]

- (1) $4.57$
- (2) $2.57$
- **(3)** $5.57$
- (4) $3.57$

**Answer** — booklet: (3) · independent solve: (3) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. $\mathrm{CH_3COONa} + \mathrm{CH_3COOH}$, $\mathrm{CH_3COOH}(\mathrm{p}K_\mathrm{a}) = 4.57$
2. $0.1\,\mathrm{M}$ $\quad$ $0.01\,\mathrm{M}$
3. $50\,\mathrm{mL}$ $\quad$ $50\,\mathrm{mL}$
4. It is a mixture of weak acid and salt of its conjugate base. Hence it is acidic buffer.
5. $\mathrm{pH} = \mathrm{p}K_\mathrm{a} + \log\frac{[\mathrm{Salt}]}{[\mathrm{Acid}]}$
6. $= 4.57 + \log\left(\frac{0.1}{0.01}\right)$
7. $= 4.57 + 1$
8. $= 5.57$

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Sodium acetate with acetic acid forms an acidic buffer, so $\mathrm{pH} = \mathrm{p}K_a + \log\frac{[\text{salt}]}{[\text{acid}]}$.
2. Equal volumes ($50\,\mathrm{mL}$ each) are mixed, so the ratio is $\frac{[\text{salt}]}{[\text{acid}]} = \frac{0.10}{0.01} = 10$.
3. $\mathrm{pH} = 4.57 + \log 10 = 4.57 + 1 = 5.57$, which is option $3$.

**Subtopic:** Buffer Solutions · **Difficulty:** medium (2 steps)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 32)

## Q78

Chemistry · organic · Basic Principles and Techniques of Organic Compounds

The Kjeldahl's method for the estimation of nitrogen can be used to estimate the amount of nitrogen in which one of the following compounds?

- **(1)** ![printed figure: option_1](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/04112026/neet_papers/2022/2022_S1/figures_agent/q078_option_1_898d91cb_04112026.png?v=898d91cb52ea) — (structure diagram — see option_boxes)
- (2) ![printed figure: option_2](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/04112026/neet_papers/2022/2022_S1/figures_agent/q078_option_2_4b26bb63_04112026.png?v=4b26bb63c1ce) — (structure diagram — see option_boxes)
- (3) ![printed figure: option_3](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/04112026/neet_papers/2022/2022_S1/figures_agent/q078_option_3_af7edf43_04112026.png?v=af7edf437348) — (structure diagram — see option_boxes)
- (4) ![printed figure: option_4](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/04112026/neet_papers/2022/2022_S1/figures_agent/q078_option_4_a83d381a_04112026.png?v=a83d381a36a4) — (structure diagram — see option_boxes)

**Answer** — booklet: (1) · independent solve: (1) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Kjeldahl method is not applicable to compounds containing nitrogen in nitro group, azo groups and nitrogen present in the ring (e.g., pyridine) as nitrogen of these compounds does not change to ammonium sulphate under these conditions.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Kjeldahl's method cannot be used for nitro compounds, azo compounds or nitrogen present in a ring, since that N is not converted to $(\mathrm{NH_4})_2\mathrm{SO_4}$.
2. Option $2$ is azobenzene ($-N=N-$), option $3$ nitrobenzene ($-\mathrm{NO_2}$), option $4$ pyridine (ring N): all excluded.
3. Option $1$ is aniline ($\mathrm{C_6H_5NH_2}$), whose N is fully converted to ammonium sulfate, so option $1$.

**Subtopic:** Quantitative Analysis · **Difficulty:** medium (2 steps)

## Q79

Chemistry · inorganic · The s-Block Elements

Identify the incorrect statement from the following

- (1) Ionisation enthalpy of alkali metals decreases from top to bottom in the group.
- (2) Lithium is the strongest reducing agent among the alkali metals.
- (3) Alkali metals react with water to form their hydroxides.
- **(4)** The oxidation number of K in $\mathrm{KO_2}$ is $+4$.

**Answer** — booklet: (4) · independent solve: (4) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Alkali metals show only '+1' oxidation state in all of their compounds.
2. $\mathrm{KO_2}$ is a super-oxide in which $\mathrm{O_2^-}$ is anion and $\mathrm{K^+}$ is cation oxidation state of K is $+1$.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Check each statement about alkali metals against known trends.
2. Ionisation enthalpy decreasing down the group (option 1) is correct, as atomic size increases and valence electron is more shielded.
3. Lithium being the strongest reducing agent (option 2) is correct due to its high hydration enthalpy despite high ionisation enthalpy.
4. Alkali metals reacting with water to form hydroxides and hydrogen gas (option 3) is correct, a well-known property.
5. In $\mathrm{KO_2}$, potassium exists as $K^+$ and oxygen as superoxide ion $O_2^-$ (charge -1), so oxidation number of K is $+1$, not $+4$.
6. Hence, option 4 is the incorrect statement.

**Subtopic:** Alkali Metals: Properties and Trends · **Difficulty:** medium (2 steps)

## Q80

Chemistry · inorganic · The p–Block Elements

Which of the following statement is not correct about diborane?

- (1) The four terminal Hydrogen atoms and the two Boron atoms lie in one plane.
- **(2)** Both the Boron atoms are $sp^{2}$ hybridised.
- (3) There are two 3-centre-2-electron bonds.
- (4) The four terminal B-H bonds are two centre two electron bonds.

**Answer** — booklet: (2) · independent solve: (2) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Each boron atoms in diborane uses $sp^{3}$ hybrid orbitals for bonding.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. In $\mathrm{B_2H_6}$ there are two bridging $\mathrm{B-H-B}$ bonds, each a 3-centre-2-electron bond, so option $3$ is correct.
2. The four terminal $\mathrm{B-H}$ bonds are ordinary 2-centre-2-electron bonds, so option $4$ is correct.
3. The four terminal H atoms and the two B atoms lie in one plane, with the bridging H atoms above and below it, so option $1$ is correct.
4. Each B uses four orbitals (two terminal and two bridging bonds), so B is $sp^{3}$ hybridised, not $sp^{2}$; option $2$ is the incorrect statement.

**Subtopic:** Important compounds of Boranes · **Difficulty:** medium (2 steps)

## Q81

Chemistry · physical · Surface Chemistry

Given below are two statements
In the light of the above statements, choose the most **appropriate** answer from the options given below

- (Statement I) In the coagulation of a negative sol, the flocculating power of the three given ions is in the order $\mathrm{Al^{3+}} > \mathrm{Ba^{2+}} > \mathrm{Na^{+}}$
- (Statement II) In the coagulation of a positive sol, the flocculating power of the three given salts is in the order $\mathrm{NaCl} > \mathrm{Na_2SO_4} > \mathrm{Na_3PO_4}$

- **(1)** Statement I is correct but Statement II is incorrect.
- (2) Statement I is incorrect but Statement II is correct.
- (3) Both Statement I and Statement II are correct.
- (4) Both Statement I and Statement II are incorrect.

**Answer** — booklet: (1) · independent solve: (1) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. According to hardy Schulze rule
2. Flocculating power of cation increases with increases in charge on cation of electrolyte in case of negatively charge colloid, hence order is
3. $\mathrm{Al^{3+}} > \mathrm{Ba^{2+}} > \mathrm{Na^{+}}$
4. Flocculating power of anion increases with increases in charge on anion of electrolyte in case of positively charge colloids
5. Hence order is
6. $\mathrm{NaCl} < \mathrm{Na_2SO_4} < \mathrm{Na_3PO_4}$

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. By the Hardy-Schulze rule, the coagulating ion carries the charge opposite to the sol, and its flocculating power increases with its charge.
2. For a negative sol the cations coagulate: $\mathrm{Al^{3+}} > \mathrm{Ba^{2+}} > \mathrm{Na^{+}}$, so Statement I is correct.
3. For a positive sol the anions coagulate: $\mathrm{PO_4^{3-}} > \mathrm{SO_4^{2-}} > \mathrm{Cl^{-}}$, so $\mathrm{Na_3PO_4} > \mathrm{Na_2SO_4} > \mathrm{NaCl}$.
4. Statement II gives the reverse order, so it is incorrect; the answer is option $1$.

**Subtopic:** Coagulation of Colloids and Hardy-Schulze Rule · **Difficulty:** medium (3 steps)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 33)

![printed question 81](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/04112026/neet_papers/2022/2022_S1/crops/q081_333519d5_04112026.png?v=333519d5ebb4)

## Q82

Chemistry · organic · Chemistry in Everyday Life

Match **List-I** with **List-II**.

| List-I | | List-II | |
|---|---|---|---|
| (a) | Antacids | (i) | Salvarsan |
| (b) | Antihistamines | (ii) | Morphine |
| (c) | Analgesics | (iii) | Cimetidine |
| (d) | Antimicrobials | (iv) | Seldane |

- (1) (a) - (i), (b) – (iv), (c) – (ii), (d) – (iii)
- (2) (a) - (iv), (b) – (iii), (c) – (i), (d) – (ii)
- (3) (a) - (iii), (b) – (ii), (c) – (iv), (d) – (i)
- **(4)** (a) - (iii), (b) – (iv), (c) – (ii), (d) – (i)

**Answer** — booklet: (4) · independent solve: (4) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Cimetidine is an antacid
2. Seldane is an antihistamine
3. Morphine is an analgesic
4. Salvarsan is an antimicrobial drug

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Antacids reduce acid secretion; Cimetidine is a classic example, so (a)-(iii).
2. Antihistamines block histamine action; Seldane is an antihistamine, so (b)-(iv).
3. Analgesics relieve pain; Morphine is a well-known analgesic, so (c)-(ii).
4. Antimicrobials act against microbes; Salvarsan is an early antimicrobial/antisyphilitic agent, so (d)-(i).
5. This matches option 4 exactly.

**Subtopic:** Therapeutic Action of Different Classes of Drugs · **Difficulty:** medium (2 steps)

What is Y in the above reaction?

## Q84

Chemistry · inorganic · The s-Block Elements

Match List-I with List-II
Choose the correct answer from the options given below :

| List-I | | List-II | |
|---|---|---|---|
| (a) | $\mathrm{Li}$ | (i) | absorbent for carbon dioxide |
| (b) | $\mathrm{Na}$ | (ii) | electrochemical cells |
| (c) | $\mathrm{KOH}$ | (iii) | coolant in fast breeder reactors |
| (d) | $\mathrm{Cs}$ | (iv) | photoelectric cell |

- (1) (a) - (i), (b) – (iii), (c) – (iv), (d) – (ii)
- **(2)** (a) - (ii), (b) – (iii), (c) – (i), (d) – (iv)
- (3) (a) - (iv), (b) – (i), (c) – (iii), (d) – (ii)
- (4) (a) - (iii), (b) – (iv), (c) – (ii), (d) – (i)

**Answer** — booklet: (2) · independent solve: (2) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. $\mathrm{Cs}$ is used in photoelectric cell due to its low ionisation enthalpy
2. $\mathrm{KOH}$ used to adsorb $\mathrm{CO_2}$ and changes into $\mathrm{K_2CO_3}$
3. Liquid sodium metal is used as a coolant in fast breeder nuclear reactor
4. Lithium is used in electrochemical cells

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. $\mathrm{Li}$ is used in electrochemical cells (lithium batteries), so (a) $\to$ (ii).
2. Liquid $\mathrm{Na}$ is a coolant in fast breeder reactors, so (b) $\to$ (iii).
3. $\mathrm{KOH}$ absorbs $\mathrm{CO_2}$ ($2\mathrm{KOH} + \mathrm{CO_2} \to \mathrm{K_2CO_3} + \mathrm{H_2O}$), so (c) $\to$ (i).
4. $\mathrm{Cs}$ has a very low ionisation enthalpy and is used in photoelectric cells, so (d) $\to$ (iv).
5. The match (a)-(ii), (b)-(iii), (c)-(i), (d)-(iv) is option $2$.

**Subtopic:** Alkali Metals: Properties and Trends · **Difficulty:** easy (2 steps)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 34)

## Q85

Chemistry · organic · General Organic Chemistry

Which compound amongst the following is **not** an aromatic compound?

- (1) ![printed figure: option_1](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/04112026/neet_papers/2022/2022_S1/figures_agent/q085_option_1_7e902f0b_04112026.png?v=7e902f0be002)
- **(2)** ![printed figure: option_2](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/04112026/neet_papers/2022/2022_S1/figures_agent/q085_option_2_aef15206_04112026.png?v=aef15206082e)
- (3) ![printed figure: option_3](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/04112026/neet_papers/2022/2022_S1/figures_agent/q085_option_3_1f603c47_04112026.png?v=1f603c47618e)
- (4) ![printed figure: option_4](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/04112026/neet_papers/2022/2022_S1/figures_agent/q085_option_4_57f17e5a_04112026.png?v=57f17e5af6cf)

**Answer** — booklet: (2) · independent solve: (2) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Planar, cyclic, conjugated species containing $(4n + 2)\pi$ electrons will be aromatic in nature ($n$ is an integer)
2. , and are aromatic species
3. is not an aromatic compound

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Option $2$ is cycloheptatriene: one ring carbon is $sp^3$ ($\mathrm{CH_2}$), so the ring is not fully conjugated and cannot be aromatic.
2. Option $1$, cyclopropenyl cation, has $2\pi$ electrons ($4n+2$, $n=0$): aromatic.
3. Option $3$, cyclopentadienyl anion, has $6\pi$ electrons ($n=1$): aromatic.
4. Option $4$, tropylium cation, has $6\pi$ electrons: aromatic.
5. The non-aromatic compound is option $2$.

## Q86

Chemistry · physical · States of Matter

A $10.0\,\mathrm{L}$ flask contains $64\,\mathrm{g}$ of oxygen at $27^\circ\mathrm{C}$. (Assume $\mathrm{O_2}$ gas is behaving ideally). The pressure inside the flask in bar is (Given $R = 0.0831\,\mathrm{L\ bar\ K^{-1}\ mol^{-1}}$)

- (1) $49.8$
- **(2)** $4.9$
- (3) $2.5$
- (4) $498.6$

**Answer** — booklet: (2) · independent solve: (2) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. We know for ideal gas
2. $\mathrm{PV} = \mathrm{nRT}$
3. $\mathrm{P} = \mathrm{n}\frac{\mathrm{RT}}{\mathrm{V}}$
4. $\mathrm{P} = \frac{64}{32} \times \frac{0.0831 \times 300}{10}$
5. $\mathrm{P} = 4.9\,\mathrm{bar}$
6. Pressure of $\mathrm{O_2}$ gas inside the flask $= 4.9\,\mathrm{bar}$

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. $n = \frac{64\,\mathrm{g}}{32\,\mathrm{g\,mol^{-1}}} = 2\,\mathrm{mol}$.
2. $T = 27 + 273 = 300\,\mathrm{K}$.
3. $P = \frac{nRT}{V} = \frac{2 \times 0.0831 \times 300}{10.0}$.
4. $P = \frac{49.86}{10.0} = 4.986\,\mathrm{bar} \approx 4.9\,\mathrm{bar}$, which is option $2$.

**Subtopic:** Gas Laws · **Difficulty:** medium (3 steps)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 35)

![printed question 86](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/04112026/neet_papers/2022/2022_S1/crops/q086_0932ed58_04112026.png?v=0932ed5880e8)

## Q87

Chemistry · physical · Structure of Atom

If radius of second Bohr orbit of the $\mathrm{He^{+}}$ ion is $105.8\,\mathrm{pm}$, what is the radius of third Bohr orbit of $\mathrm{Li^{2+}}$ ion?

- (1) $1.587\,\mathrm{pm}$
- (2) $158.7\,\mathrm{\AA}$
- **(3)** $158.7\,\mathrm{pm}$
- (4) $15.87\,\mathrm{pm}$

**Answer** — booklet: (3) · independent solve: (3) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. $r_n \propto \frac{n^2}{Z}$
2. $\frac{r_3(\mathrm{Li^{2+}})}{r_2(\mathrm{He^{+}})} = \frac{(n_3)^2}{Z(\mathrm{Li^{2+}})} \times \frac{Z(\mathrm{He^{+}})}{(n_2)^2}$
3. $\frac{r_3(\mathrm{Li^{2+}})}{105.8} = \frac{(3)^2}{3} \times \frac{2}{(2)^2}$
4. $= 105.8 \times \frac{3}{2}$
5. $r_3(\mathrm{Li^{2+}}) = 158.7\,\mathrm{pm}$

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Bohr radius: $r_n = 52.9 \frac{n^2}{Z}$ pm.
2. Verify given data: for $He^{+}$ ($Z=2$, $n=2$): $r = 52.9 \times 4/2 = 105.8$ pm, matches stem.
3. For $Li^{2+}$ ($Z=3$, $n=3$): $r = 52.9 \times 9/3 = 158.7$ pm.
4. This matches option 3.

**Subtopic:** Bohr’s Model for Hydrogen Atom · **Difficulty:** medium (3 steps)

## Q88

Chemistry · organic · IUPAC Nomenclature of Organic Chemistry

The correct IUPAC name of the following compound is

![printed figure: question](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/04112026/neet_papers/2022/2022_S1/figures_agent/q088_ba91ee99_04112026.png?v=ba91ee99d908)

- (1) 1-bromo-4-methyl-5-chlorohexan-3-ol
- (2) 6-bromo-4-methyl-2-chlorohexan-4-ol
- **(3)** 1-bromo-5-chloro-4-methylhexan-3-ol
- (4) 6-bromo-2-chloro-4-methylhexan-4-ol

**Answer** — booklet: (3) · independent solve: (3) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. $1$-bromo-$5$-chloro-$4$-methylhexan-$3$-ol

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. The longest chain containing $\ce{-OH}$ runs from the $\ce{CH2Br}$ carbon to the terminal $\ce{CH3}$ next to $\ce{CHCl}$: $6$ carbons, so the parent is hexan-ol.
2. Numbering from the Br end puts $\ce{-OH}$ at $C_3$; from the other end it would be $C_4$, so number from the Br end.
3. Substituents then sit at: Br on $C_1$, $\ce{CH3}$ on $C_4$, Cl on $C_5$.
4. Prefixes in alphabetical order: bromo, chloro, methyl $\Rightarrow$ 1-bromo-5-chloro-4-methylhexan-3-ol, which is option $3$.

**Subtopic:** Nomenclature of Organic Compounds Having Functional Group(s) · **Difficulty:** hard (4 steps)

## Q89

Chemistry · physical · Chemical Equilibrium

$\mathrm{3O_2(g)}\;\rightleftharpoons\;\mathrm{2O_3(g)}$

for the above reaction at $298\,\mathrm{K}$, $\mathrm{K_C}$ is found to be $3.0 \times 10^{-59}$. If the concentration of $\mathrm{O_2}$ at equilibrium is $0.040\,\mathrm{M}$ then concentration of $\mathrm{O_3}$ in M is

- (1) $2.4 \times 10^{31}$
- (2) $1.2 \times 10^{21}$
- **(3)** $4.38 \times 10^{-32}$
- (4) $1.9 \times 10^{-63}$

**Answer** — booklet: (3) · independent solve: (3) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. $\mathrm{3O_2(g)}\;\rightleftharpoons\;\mathrm{2O_3(g)}$
2. $\mathrm{K_C} = \frac{[\mathrm{O_3}]^2}{[\mathrm{O_2}]^3}$
3. $[\mathrm{O_3}]^2 = \mathrm{K_C}[\mathrm{O_2}]^3 = 3 \times 10^{-59} \times (0.04)^3$
4. $[\mathrm{O_3}]^2 = 1.9 \times 10^{-63} = 19 \times 10^{-64}$
5. $[\mathrm{O_3}] = 4.38 \times 10^{-32}$
6. Concentration of $\mathrm{O_3}$ at equilibrium $= 4.38 \times 10^{-32}\,\mathrm{M}$

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. $K_C = \frac{[\mathrm{O_3}]^2}{[\mathrm{O_2}]^3}$.
2. $[\mathrm{O_2}]^3 = (0.040)^3 = 6.4 \times 10^{-5}$.
3. $[\mathrm{O_3}]^2 = 3.0 \times 10^{-59} \times 6.4 \times 10^{-5} = 1.92 \times 10^{-63} = 19.2 \times 10^{-64}$.
4. $[\mathrm{O_3}] = \sqrt{19.2} \times 10^{-32} \approx 4.38 \times 10^{-32}\,\mathrm{M}$, which is option $3$.

**Subtopic:** Calculating Equilibrium Concentrations · **Difficulty:** hard (4 steps)

- **(4)** ![printed figure: option_4](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/04112026/neet_papers/2022/2022_S1/figures_agent/q090_option_4_27207278_04112026.png?v=272072784ebc)

**Answer** — booklet: (4) · independent solve: (4) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Cross Aldol condensation reaction:
2. Both reactants contain $\alpha$-Hydrogens, so multiple products are possible which are as follows:
3. $\Rightarrow$ (4) is not possible.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Identify enolisable alpha-carbons: acetone has one type (CH3), 2-pentanone has two types (C1-CH3 and C3-CH2).
2. Acetone enolate attacking 2-pentanone's carbonyl gives, after dehydration, CH3-CO-CH=C(CH3)-CH2CH2CH3 — this is option 1, a valid product.
3. 2-Pentanone's C1-enolate attacking acetone's carbonyl gives, after dehydration, CH3CH2-CO-CH=C(CH3)2 — this is option 3, a valid product.
4. 2-Pentanone's C3-enolate attacking acetone's carbonyl gives, after dehydration, (CH3)2C=C(C2H5)-CO-CH3 — this is option 2, a valid product.
5. Since all enolate/carbonyl combinations between acetone and 2-pentanone are exhausted by options 1, 2 and 3, the structure in option 4 cannot arise from this cross-aldol condensation and is therefore the one not formed.

## Q91

Chemistry · physical · Electrochemistry

> ⚠ The booklet prints "Answer (NA)": none of the four options is correct as printed, so there is no keyed answer to check a solve against.

> ⚠ The independent solve found no printed option exactly right ("No option matches the printed values: with E°cell = 10"); its pick (unsure) is the closest fit, so treat this question as possibly misprinted.

Find the emf of the cell in which the following reaction takes place at 298 K
$\mathrm{Ni(s)} + 2\mathrm{Ag}^{+}\,(0.001\,\mathrm{M}) \rightarrow \mathrm{Ni}^{2+}\,(0.001\,\mathrm{M}) + 2\mathrm{Ag(s)}$
$\left(\text{Given that } \mathrm{E}^{\circ}_{\mathrm{cell}} = 10.5\,\mathrm{V},\ \dfrac{2.303\,RT}{F} = 0.059 \text{ at } 298\,\mathrm{K}\right)$

- (1) $0.9615\,\mathrm{V}$
- (2) $1.05\,\mathrm{V}$
- (3) $1.0385\,\mathrm{V}$
- (4) $1.385\,\mathrm{V}$

**Answer** — booklet: (none) · independent solve: (unsure) · publisher_no_valid_option

**Publisher's solution**

1. $\mathrm{Ni(s)} + 2\mathrm{Ag}^{+}\,(0.001\,\mathrm{M}) \rightarrow \mathrm{Ni}^{2+}\,(0.001\,\mathrm{M}) + 2\mathrm{Ag(s)}$
2. $\mathrm{E}^{\circ}_{\mathrm{cell}} = 10.5\,\mathrm{V}$
3. $\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{cell}} = \mathrm{E}^{\circ}_{\mathrm{cell}} - \frac{0.059}{\mathrm{n}}\log\frac{\left[\mathrm{Ni}^{2+}\right]}{\left[\mathrm{Ag}^{+}\right]^{2}}$
4. $= 10.5 - \frac{0.059}{2}\log\frac{\left(10^{-3}\right)}{\left(10^{-3}\right)^{2}}$
5. $\Rightarrow 10.5 - \frac{0.059}{2}\log\left(10\right)^{3}$
6. $\Rightarrow 10.5 - 0.0295 \times 3$
7. $= 10.5 - 0.0885$
8. $= 10.4115\,\mathrm{V}$

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. The reaction transfers $n = 2$ electrons.
2. $Q = \frac{[\mathrm{Ni^{2+}}]}{[\mathrm{Ag^{+}}]^2} = \frac{0.001}{(0.001)^2} = 10^{3}$.
3. $\log Q = 3$.
4. $E = E^{\circ}_{\mathrm{cell}} - \frac{0.059}{2}\log Q = 10.5 - 0.0295 \times 3$.
5. $E = 10.5 - 0.0885 = 10.4115\,\mathrm{V}$.
6. No option equals $10.4115\,\mathrm{V}$, so no option matches.

**Subtopic:** Nernst Equation · **Difficulty:** medium (3 steps)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 37)

## Q92

Chemistry · inorganic · The D- and F-Block Elements – Practice

In the neutral or faintly alkaline medium, $\mathrm{KMnO_4}$ oxidises iodide into iodate. The change in oxidation state of manganese in this reaction is from

- (1) $+7$ to $+3$
- (2) $+6$ to $+5$
- **(3)** $+7$ to $+4$
- (4) $+6$ to $+4$

**Answer** — booklet: (3) · independent solve: (3) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. In neutral or faintly alkaline solution.
2. $2\overset{+7}{\mathrm{Mn}}\mathrm{O_4^-} + \mathrm{H_2O} + \overset{-1}{\mathrm{I^-}}\;(\text{iodide}) \rightarrow 2\overset{+4}{\mathrm{Mn}}\mathrm{O_2} + 2\mathrm{OH^-} + \overset{+5}{\mathrm{I}}\mathrm{O_3^-}\;(\text{iodate})$
3. Manganese $(\mathrm{Mn})$ oxidation state change from $+7$ to $+4$.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. In neutral or faintly alkaline medium, $\mathrm{MnO_4^-}$ is reduced to $\mathrm{MnO_2}$: $2\mathrm{MnO_4^-} + \mathrm{I^-} + \mathrm{H_2O} \rightarrow 2\mathrm{MnO_2} + \mathrm{IO_3^-} + 2\mathrm{OH^-}$.
2. Oxidation state of Mn in $\mathrm{KMnO_4}$: $+1 + x + 4(-2) = 0 \Rightarrow x = +7$.
3. Oxidation state of Mn in $\mathrm{MnO_2}$: $x + 2(-2) = 0 \Rightarrow x = +4$.
4. Change is $+7$ to $+4$, which is option $3$.

**Subtopic:** Potassium Permanganate (KMnO4) · **Difficulty:** medium (2 steps)

## Q93

Chemistry · physical · Chemical Kinetics

For a first order reaction $\mathrm{A} \rightarrow$ Products, initial concentration of $\mathrm{A}$ is $0.1\,\mathrm{M}$, which becomes $0.001\,\mathrm{M}$ after 5 minutes. Rate constant for the reaction in $\mathrm{min^{-1}}$ is

- (1) $0.4606$
- (2) $0.2303$
- (3) $1.3818$
- **(4)** $0.9212$

**Answer** — booklet: (4) · independent solve: (4) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. For first order reaction,
2. $\mathrm{K} = \frac{2.303}{\mathrm{t}}\log\frac{[\mathrm{A_0}]}{[\mathrm{A}]}$;   where $\mathrm{A_0}$ is the initial concentration of reactant A.
3. $\mathrm{A_0} = 0.1\,\mathrm{M}$
4. $\mathrm{A} = 0.001\,\mathrm{M}$
5. $\mathrm{t} = 5$ minute
6. $\mathrm{K} = \frac{2.303}{5}\log\frac{0.1}{0.001} = \frac{2.303}{5}\log 10^{2}$
7. $= \frac{2.303}{5}\times 2$
8. $\mathrm{K} = 0.9212\,\mathrm{min^{-1}}$

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. For a first order reaction, $k = \frac{2.303}{t}\log\frac{[A]_0}{[A]}$.
2. $\frac{[A]_0}{[A]} = \frac{0.1}{0.001} = 100$, so $\log 100 = 2$.
3. $k = \frac{2.303}{5} \times 2 = \frac{4.606}{5} = 0.9212\,\mathrm{min^{-1}}$, which is option $4$.

**Subtopic:** First Order Reactions · **Difficulty:** medium (3 steps)

Compound X on reaction with $\mathrm{O_3}$ followed by $\mathrm{Zn/H_2O}$ gives formaldehyde and 2-methyl propanal as products. The compound X is

- (1) 2-Methylbut-2-ene
- (2) Pent-2-ene
- **(3)** 3-Methylbut-1-ene
- (4) 2-Methylbut-1-ene

**Answer** — booklet: (3) · independent solve: (3) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. The given reaction is the reductive ozonolysis of an alkene. The alkene will be

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Reductive ozonolysis splits the $\mathrm{C=C}$ bond and turns each alkene carbon into a $\mathrm{C=O}$ group.
2. Formaldehyde $\mathrm{HCHO}$ comes from a terminal $\mathrm{=CH_2}$ group, and 2-methylpropanal $\mathrm{(CH_3)_2CH{-}CHO}$ comes from a $\mathrm{=CH{-}CH(CH_3)_2}$ fragment.
3. Join the two carbonyl carbons with a double bond: $\mathrm{CH_2{=}CH{-}CH(CH_3)_2}$.
4. $\mathrm{CH_2{=}CH{-}CH(CH_3)_2}$ is 3-methylbut-1-ene, which is option $3$.

## Q96

Chemistry · physical · The Solid State

Copper crystallises in fcc unit cell with cell edge length of $3.608 \times 10^{-8}\,\mathrm{cm}$. The density of copper is $8.92\,\mathrm{g\,cm^{-3}}$. Calculate the atomic mass of copper.

- (1) $60\,\mathrm{u}$
- (2) $65\,\mathrm{u}$
- **(3)** $63.1\,\mathrm{u}$
- (4) $31.55\,\mathrm{u}$

**Answer** — booklet: (3) · independent solve: (3) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. $d = \frac{ZM}{N_A (a)^3}$
2. $Z = 4(\mathrm{FCC})$, $d = 8.92\,\mathrm{g\,cm^{-3}}$, $N_A = 6.023 \times 10^{23}$, $a = 3.608 \times 10^{-8}\,\mathrm{cm}$
3. $M = \frac{dN_A (a)^3}{Z}$
4. $= \frac{8.92 \times 6.023 \times 10^{23} \times (3.608 \times 10^{-8})^3}{4}$
5. $= \frac{8.92 \times 6.023 \times 10^{23} \times 46.97 \times 10^{-24}}{4} = \frac{2523.47 \times 10^{-1}}{4}$
6. $= 630.8 \times 10^{-1} = 63.08 \simeq 63.1\,\mathrm{u}$

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. For an fcc unit cell, $Z = 4$ atoms per cell.
2. Density relation: $d = \frac{Z M}{a^3 N_A}$, so $M = \frac{d\,a^3 N_A}{Z}$.
3. $a^3 = (3.608 \times 10^{-8}\,\mathrm{cm})^3 = 46.97 \times 10^{-24}\,\mathrm{cm^3}$.
4. $d\,a^3 = 8.92 \times 46.97 \times 10^{-24} = 418.96 \times 10^{-24}\,\mathrm{g}$.
5. $d\,a^3 N_A = 418.96 \times 10^{-24} \times 6.022 \times 10^{23} = 252.3\,\mathrm{g\,mol^{-1}}$.
6. $M = \frac{252.3}{4} = 63.07 \approx 63.1\,\mathrm{u}$, which is option $3$.

**Subtopic:** Density and Edge Length Calculations for Cubic Unit Cells · **Difficulty:** medium (3 steps)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 39)

![printed question 96](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/04112026/neet_papers/2022/2022_S1/crops/q096_71877744_04112026.png?v=718777442436)

Publisher's solution, as printed:

![printed solution 96](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/04112026/neet_papers/2022/2022_S1/solutions/q096_08c2e974_04112026.png?v=08c2e97457c3)

## Q97

Chemistry · organic · Alcohols, Phenols and Ethers

Given below are two statements:
In the light of the above statements, choose the most appropriate answer from the options given below:

- (Statement I) In Lucas test, primary, secondary and tertiary alcohols are distinguished on the basis of their reactivity with conc. $\mathrm{HCl}$ + $\mathrm{ZnCl_2}$, known as Lucas Reagent.
- (Statement II) Primary alcohols are most reactive and immediately produce turbidity at room temperature on reaction with Lucas Reagent.

- **(1)** Statement I is correct but Statement II is incorrect
- (2) Statement I is incorrect but Statement II is correct
- (3) Both Statement I and Statement II are correct
- (4) Both Statement I and Statement II are incorrect

**Answer** — booklet: (1) · independent solve: (1) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Primary, secondary and tertiary alcohols can be differentiated by their reaction with ($\mathrm{HCl}$ + anhy $\mathrm{ZnCl_2}$) Lucas reagent
2. $3^\circ$ alcohol $\xrightarrow{\mathrm{ZnCl_2 + HCl}}$ Immediate turbidity at room temperature
3. $2^\circ$ alcohol $\xrightarrow{\mathrm{ZnCl_2 + HCl}}$ Turbidity after $5$ minutes at room temperature
4. $1^\circ$ alcohol $\xrightarrow{\mathrm{ZnCl_2 + HCl}}$ Do not gives turbidity at room temperature

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Statement I: Lucas reagent (conc. $\mathrm{HCl} + \mathrm{ZnCl_2}$) distinguishes $1^\circ$, $2^\circ$ and $3^\circ$ alcohols by their rate of reaction, so it is correct.
2. Statement II: reactivity order is $3^\circ > 2^\circ > 1^\circ$ (carbocation stability); tertiary alcohols give immediate turbidity, primary alcohols give none at room temperature, so it is incorrect.
3. Statement I correct and Statement II incorrect is option $1$.

**Subtopic:** Test of Alcohol · **Difficulty:** medium (3 steps)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 40)

- (3) ![printed figure: option_3](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/04112026/neet_papers/2022/2022_S1/figures_agent/q098_option_3_a486f6ab_04112026.png?v=a486f6abf2ba)
- (4) ![printed figure: option_4](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/04112026/neet_papers/2022/2022_S1/figures_agent/q098_option_4_cb381a95_04112026.png?v=cb381a950d3f)

**Answer** — booklet: (2) · independent solve: (2) · agree

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Step 1: LiAlH4 reduces the nitrile group (-C≡N) fully to a primary amine: PhCN → PhCH2NH2 (benzylamine).
2. Step 2: NaNO2 + HCl generates nitrous acid (HNO2) in situ, which diazotises the primary aliphatic amine to form an unstable alkyl diazonium salt, PhCH2N2+Cl-.
3. Step 3: Aliphatic diazonium salts are unstable even at low temperature and decompose immediately via an SN1-type mechanism, releasing N2 gas and forming a carbocation (PhCH2+).
4. Step 4: Water (H2O) attacks the benzylic carbocation to give the final product, benzyl alcohol (PhCH2OH), matching option 2.

**Subtopic:** Chemical Reactions of Amines (Carbylamine, Nitrous Acid, Arylsulphonyl Chloride and Electrophilic Substitution Reactions), Methods of Preparation of Amines · **Difficulty:** hard (3 steps)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 40)

![printed question 98](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/04112026/neet_papers/2022/2022_S1/crops/q098_a0b8c84f_04112026.png?v=a0b8c84fa4f2)

## Q99

Chemistry · inorganic · Coordination Compounds – Practice

The order of energy absorbed which is responsible for the color of complexes

- (A) $\mathrm{[Ni(H_2O)_2(en)_2]^{2+}}$
- (B) $\mathrm{[Ni(H_2O)_4(en)]^{2+}}$ and
- (C) $\mathrm{[Ni(en)_3]^{2+}}$

- **(1)** (C) > (A) > (B)
- (2) (B) > (A) > (C)
- (3) (A) > (B) > (C)
- (4) (C) > (B) > (A)

**Answer** — booklet: (1) · independent solve: (1) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Stronger the field strength of ligand, higher will be the energy absorbed by the complex.
2. $\Rightarrow$ 'en' has a stronger field strength than '$\mathrm{H_2O}$' according to spectrochemical series
3. $\therefore$ Correct order of energy absorbed will be:
4. $\mathrm{[Ni(en)_3]^{2+}} > \mathrm{[Ni(H_2O)_2(en)_2]^{2+}} > \mathrm{[Ni(H_2O)_4(en)]^{2+}}$
5. i.e. $(C) > (A) > (B)$

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Ethylenediamine (en) is a bidentate, stronger field ligand than H2O in the spectrochemical series.
2. More en ligands in the complex increase the crystal field splitting energy (Δ), leading to absorption of higher energy light.
3. Comparing en content: C has 3 en, A has 2 en, B has 1 en, so Δ and energy absorbed order is C > A > B.
4. This matches option 1: (C) > (A) > (B).

**Subtopic:** Colour in Coordination Compounds · **Difficulty:** medium (3 steps)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 41)

![printed question 99](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/04112026/neet_papers/2022/2022_S1/crops/q099_fb4c3f39_04112026.png?v=fb4c3f39c216)

Publisher's solution, as printed:

![printed solution 99](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/04112026/neet_papers/2022/2022_S1/solutions/q099_5d91751e_04112026.png?v=5d91751eb26a)

## Q100

Chemistry · inorganic · Environmental Chemistry

The pollution due to oxides of sulphur gets enhanced due to the presence of:
Choose the most appropriate answer from the options given below:

- (a) particulate matter
- (b) ozone
- (c) hydrocarbons
- (d) hydrogen peroxide

- (1) (b), (c), (d) only
- (2) (a), (c), (d) only
- (3) (a), (d) only
- **(4)** (a), (b), (d) only

**Answer** — booklet: (4) · independent solve: (4) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Presence of particulate matter in polluted air catalyzes the oxidation of $\mathrm{SO_2}$ to $\mathrm{SO_3}$
2. i.e., $2\mathrm{SO_2}\left(\mathrm{g}\right)+\mathrm{O_2}\left(\mathrm{g}\right)\longrightarrow 2\mathrm{SO_3}\left(\mathrm{g}\right)$
3. This reaction can also be promoted by $\mathrm{O_3}$ and $\mathrm{H_2O_2}$, as
4. $\mathrm{SO_2}\left(\mathrm{g}\right)+\mathrm{O_3}\left(\mathrm{g}\right)\longrightarrow \mathrm{SO_3}\left(\mathrm{g}\right)+\mathrm{O_2}\left(\mathrm{g}\right)$
5. $\mathrm{SO_2}\left(\mathrm{g}\right)+\mathrm{H_2O_2}\left(\mathrm{l}\right)\longrightarrow \mathrm{H_2SO_4}\left(\mathrm{aq}\right)$

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Particulate matter in polluted air catalyses the oxidation $\mathrm{2SO_2 + O_2 \rightarrow 2SO_3}$, so (a) enhances the pollution.
2. Ozone ($\mathrm{O_3}$) and hydrogen peroxide ($\mathrm{H_2O_2}$) also promote the oxidation of $\mathrm{SO_2}$ to $\mathrm{SO_3}$, so (b) and (d) enhance it.
3. Hydrocarbons are not an agent of $\mathrm{SO_2}$ oxidation, so (c) is excluded.
4. The set (a), (b), (d) only is option $4$.

**Subtopic:** Air Pollution · **Difficulty:** hard (4 steps)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 41)

![printed question 100](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/04112026/neet_papers/2022/2022_S1/crops/q100_8ebb9b73_04112026.png?v=8ebb9b7355c8)

Publisher's solution, as printed:

![printed solution 100](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/04112026/neet_papers/2022/2022_S1/solutions/q100_2f7e80b6_04112026.png?v=2f7e80b65fc0)

## Q101

Biology · botany · Transport in Plants

Which of the following is **not** observed during apoplastic pathway ?

- **(1)** The movement is aided by cytoplasmic streaming
- (2) Apoplast is continuous and does not provide any barrier to water movement
- (3) Movement of water occurs through intercellular spaces and wall of the cells
- (4) The movement does not involve crossing of cell membrane

**Answer** — booklet: (1) · independent solve: (1) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. The symplastic system is system of interconnected protoplasts.
2. Neighbouring cells are connected through cytoplasmic strands that extend through plasmodesmata.
3. The water travels through cell cytoplasm and plasmodesmata, hence the movement is relatively slower.
4. Symplastic movement is aided by cytoplasmic streaming.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. The apoplastic pathway moves water through cell walls and intercellular spaces without crossing any membrane, and the apoplast is continuous, so options $3$, $4$ and $2$ are observed; cytoplasmic streaming aids only the symplastic pathway, so option $1$ is not observed.

**Subtopic:** Water Movement up a Plant: Root Pressure and Guttation · **Difficulty:** medium (2 steps)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 42)

![printed question 101](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/04112026/neet_papers/2022/2022_S1/crops/q101_097d7325_04112026.png?v=097d7325e737)

## Q102

Biology · botany · Anatomy of Flowering Plants

In old trees the greater part of secondary xylem is dark brown and resistant to insect attack due to :

Choose the **correct** answer from the options given below:

- (a) secretion of secondary metabolities and their deposition in the lumen of vessels.
- (b) deposition of organic compounds like tannins and resins in the central layers of stem.
- (c) deposition of suberin and aromatic substances in the outer layer of stem.
- (d) deposition of tannins, gum, resin and aromatic substances in the peripheral layers of stem.
- (e) presence of parenchyma cells, functionally active xylem elements and essential oils.

- (1) (d) and (e) Only
- (2) (b) and (d) Only
- **(3)** (a) and (b) Only
- (4) (c) and (d) Only

**Answer** — booklet: (3) · independent solve: (3) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. In old trees, the greater part of secondary xylem is dark brown due to deposition of organic compounds like tannins, resins, oils, gums, aromatic substances and essential oils in the central or innermost layers of the stem.
2. These substances make it hard, durable and resistant to the attacks of micro-organisms and insects.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. The dark, insect-resistant inner part of old secondary xylem is heartwood.
2. Heartwood is dark because organic compounds such as tannins, resins, oils, gums and aromatic substances (secondary metabolites) are deposited in it, filling the lumen of its vessels, so $(a)$ and $(b)$ are true.
3. These deposits are in the central (innermost) layers, not the outer or peripheral layers, so $(c)$ and $(d)$ are false.
4. Living parenchyma and functionally active xylem elements describe sapwood, so $(e)$ is false.
5. Only $(a)$ and $(b)$ are correct, which is option $3$.

**Subtopic:** Secondary Growth · **Difficulty:** hard (4 steps)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 42)

![printed question 102](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/04112026/neet_papers/2022/2022_S1/crops/q102_492f5b76_04112026.png?v=492f5b76120d)

Publisher's solution, as printed:

![printed solution 102](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/04112026/neet_papers/2022/2022_S1/solutions/q102_13e1aed2_04112026.png?v=13e1aed28164)

## Q103

Biology · botany · Organisms and Populations

Which one of the following statements cannot be connected to Predation?

- **(1)** Both the interacting species are negatively impacted
- (2) It is necessitated by nature to maintain the ecological balance
- (3) It helps in maintaining species diversity in a community
- (4) It might lead to extinction of a species

**Answer** — booklet: (1) · independent solve: (1) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. One of the species in predation gains benefit on the expense of the other.
2. Predators help in maintaining species diversity in a community, by reducing the intensity of competition among competing prey species.
3. If a predator is too efficient and overexploits its prey, then the prey might become extinct.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Predation is a $(+,-)$ interaction: the predator gains and the prey is harmed; an interaction where both species are harmed $(-,-)$ is competition, so option $1$ cannot be connected to predation.

**Subtopic:** Predation · **Difficulty:** medium (2 steps)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 42)

![printed question 103](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/04112026/neet_papers/2022/2022_S1/crops/q103_5521b108_04112026.png?v=5521b108e079)

## Q104

Biology · botany · Morphology of Flowering Plants

The flowers are Zygomorphic in:
Choose the **correct answer** from the options given below:

- (a) Mustard
- (b) Gulmohar
- (c) Cassia
- (d) Datura
- (e) Chilly

- (1) (d), (e) Only
- (2) (c), (d), (e) Only
- (3) (a), (b), (c) Only
- **(4)** (b), (c) Only

**Answer** — booklet: (4) · independent solve: (4) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. When a flower can be divided into two similar halves only in one particular vertical plane, it is zygomorphic for e.g. pea, gulmohar, bean, $Cassia$. Mustard, $Datura$ and Chilli show actinomorphic flowers.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. NCERT Class 11 (Morphology of Flowering Plants) gives mustard, datura and chilli as examples of actinomorphic flowers, so (a), (d) and (e) are not zygomorphic.
2. The same text gives pea, gulmohur, bean and Cassia as examples of zygomorphic flowers, so (b) and (c) are zygomorphic.
3. Only (b) and (c) are zygomorphic, which is option $4$.

**Subtopic:** The Flower · **Difficulty:** medium (2 steps)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 43)

## Q105

Biology · botany · Molecular Basis of Inheritance

The process of translation of mRNA to proteins begins as soon as :

- (1) Both the subunits join together to bind with mRNA
- (2) The tRNA is activated and the larger subunit of ribosome encounters mRNA
- **(3)** The small subunit of ribosome encounters mRNA
- (4) The larger subunit of ribosome encounters mRNA

**Answer** — booklet: (3) · independent solve: (3) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. When the small subunit of ribosome encounters an mRNA, the process of translation of the mRNA to protein begins. This process is followed by the binding of bigger/larger subunit.
2. t-RNA is activated by the addition of amino acid prior to the attachment of ribosome, in the first phase.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. An inactive ribosome exists as two separate subunits, and translation of mRNA into protein begins as soon as the small subunit encounters the mRNA, which is option $3$.

**Subtopic:** Translation · **Difficulty:** medium (1 step)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 43)

## Q106

Biology · botany · Morphology of Flowering Plants

Which one of the following plants shows vexillary aestivation and diadelphous stamens?

- (1) Allium cepa
- (2) Solanum nigrum
- (3) Colchicum autumnale
- **(4)** Pisum sativum

**Answer** — booklet: (4) · independent solve: (4) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Vexillary aestivation and diadelphous stamens are the characteristic features of family Fabaceae.
2. $\textit{Pisum sativum}$ (garden pea) belongs to family Fabaceae.
3. $\textit{Allium cepa}$ (onion) and $\textit{Colchicum autumnale}$ (colchicine) belong to family Liliaceae.
4. $\textit{Solanum nigrum}$ belongs to Solanaceae.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Vexillary aestivation and diadelphous stamens $(9)+1$ are characteristic of Fabaceae; Pisum sativum belongs to Fabaceae, while Colchicum and Allium are Liliaceae and Solanum is Solanaceae, so the answer is option $4$.

**Subtopic:** Leguminosae, Compositae, Gramineae and Fabaceae · **Difficulty:** easy (1 step)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 43)

## Q107

Biology · botany · Sexual Reproduction in Flowering Plants

Identify the **incorrect** statement related to Pollination :

- (1) Flowers produce foul odours to attract flies and beetles to get pollinated
- **(2)** Moths and butterflies are the most dominant pollinating agents among insects
- (3) Pollination by water is quite rare in flowering plants
- (4) Pollination by wind is more common amongst abiotic pollination

**Answer** — booklet: (2) · independent solve: (2) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Among the animals, insects, particularly bees are the dominant biotic pollinating agents.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Statement 1: pollination by water is quite rare in flowering plants (about 30 genera, mostly monocots) $\Rightarrow$ correct.
2. Statement 2: among abiotic agents, wind pollination is more common than water pollination $\Rightarrow$ correct.
3. Statement 3: some flowers produce foul odours to attract flies and beetles for pollination $\Rightarrow$ correct.
4. Statement 4: among insects, bees are the dominant pollinating agents, not moths and butterflies $\Rightarrow$ incorrect, so the answer is option $2$.

**Subtopic:** Agents of Pollination · **Difficulty:** medium (1 step)

## Q109

Biology · botany · Molecular Basis of Inheritance

Read the following statements and choose the set of **correct** statements :

- (a) Euchromatin is loosely packed chromatin
- (b) Heterochromatin is transcriptionally active
- (c) Histone octomer is wrapped by negatively charged DNA in nucleosome
- (d) Histones are rich in lysine and arginine
- (e) A typical nucleosome contains $400\,\mathrm{bp}$ of DNA helix

- (1) (b), (e) Only
- (2) (a), (c), (e) Only
- (3) (b), (d), (e) Only
- **(4)** (a), (c), (d) Only

**Answer** — booklet: (4) · independent solve: (4) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Heterochromatin is transcriptionally inactive. A typical nucleosome contains $200\,\mathrm{bp}$ of DNA helix.
2. Euchromatin is the loosely packed chromatin region.
3. The negatively charged DNA is wrapped around the positively charged histone octamer to form a structure called nucleosome. Histones are rich in basic amino acid residues lysine and arginine.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. (a) Euchromatin is loosely packed chromatin: true.
2. (b) Heterochromatin is densely packed and transcriptionally inactive: false.
3. (c) Negatively charged DNA wraps the positively charged histone octamer in a nucleosome: true.
4. (d) Histones are rich in the basic residues lysine and arginine: true.
5. (e) A typical nucleosome contains $200\,\mathrm{bp}$ of DNA, not $400\,\mathrm{bp}$: false.
6. True set is (a), (c), (d), which is option $4$.

**Subtopic:** Packaging of DNA Helix · **Difficulty:** medium (3 steps)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 44)

## Q113

Biology · botany · Environmental Issues

The device which can remove particulate matter present in the exhaust from a thermal power plant is :

- **(1)** Electrostatic Precipitator
- (2) Catalytic Convertor
- (3) STP
- (4) Incinerator

**Answer** — booklet: (1) · independent solve: (1) · agree

2. Catalytic converters are fitted into automobiles for reducing emission of poisonous gases.
3. STPs are associated with sewage treatment.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. An electrostatic precipitator charges the dust particles in the exhaust and collects them on oppositely charged plates, removing particulate matter from thermal power plant exhaust, which is option $1$.

## Q114

Biology · botany · Mineral Nutrition

Match **List-I** with **List-II**

Choose the **correct answer** from the options given below :

| List-I | | List-II | |
|---|---|---|---|
| (a) | Manganese | (i) | Activates the enzyme catalase |
| (b) | Magnesium | (ii) | Required for pollen germination |
| (c) | Boron | (iii) | Activates enzymes of respiration |
| (d) | Iron | (iv) | Functions in splitting of water during photosynthesis |

- (1) (a)-(iv), (b)-(i), (c)-(ii), (d)-(iii)
- (2) (a)-(iii), (b)-(i), (c)-(ii), (d)-(iv)
- (3) (a)-(iii), (b)-(iv), (c)-(i), (d)-(ii)
- **(4)** (a)-(iv), (b)-(iii), (c)-(ii), (d)-(i)

**Answer** — booklet: (4) · independent solve: (4) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Manganese plays a major role in the splitting of water to liberate oxygen during photosynthesis.
2. Magnesium activates several enzymes involved in photosynthesis and respiration.
3. Boron is involved in pollen germination. Iron activates the catalase and some other enzymes.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Manganese is needed for the photolysis (splitting) of water during photosynthesis, so (a)-(iv).
2. Magnesium activates enzymes of respiration and photosynthesis, so (b)-(iii).
3. Boron is required for pollen germination, so (c)-(ii).
4. Iron activates the enzyme catalase, so (d)-(i).
5. The match (a)-(iv), (b)-(iii), (c)-(ii), (d)-(i) is option $4$.

**Subtopic:** Essential Mineral Elements: Macronutrients and Micronutrients · **Difficulty:** hard (3 steps)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 45)
