## Q115

Biology · botany · Morphology of Flowering Plants

Identify the **correct** set of statements :
Choose the **correct answer** from the options given below :

- (a) The leaflets are modified into pointed hard thorns in $Citrus$ and $Bougainvillea$
- (b) Axillary buds form slender and spirally coiled tendrils in cucumber and pumpkin
- (c) Stem is flattened and fleshy in $Opuntia$ and modified to perform the function of leaves
- (d) $Rhizophora$ shows vertically upward growing roots that help to get oxygen for respiration
- (e) Subaerially growing stems in grasses and strawberry help in vegetative propagation

- **(1)** (b), (c), (d) and (e) Only
- (2) (a), (b), (d) and (e) Only
- (3) (b) and (c) Only
- (4) (a) and (d) Only

**Answer** — booklet: (1) · independent solve: (1) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Axillary buds of stems get modified into woody, straight and pointed thorns. Thorns are found in many plants such as $Citrus$ and $Bougainvillea$.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. (a) is false: in $Citrus$ and $Bougainvillea$ the thorns are modified axillary buds (stem modification), not leaflets.
2. (b) is true: stem tendrils arising from axillary buds are slender and spirally coiled in cucumber and pumpkin.
3. (c) is true: $Opuntia$ has a flattened, fleshy green stem (phylloclade) that performs the function of leaves.
4. (d) is true: $Rhizophora$ has pneumatophores, roots growing vertically upward to obtain oxygen for respiration.
5. (e) is true: subaerially growing stems (runners/stolons) in grasses and strawberry help in vegetative propagation.
6. Correct set is (b), (c), (d) and (e) only, which is option $1$.

**Subtopic:** The Stem, The Root · **Difficulty:** hard (6 steps)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 45)

## Q116

Biology · botany · Principles of Inheritance and Variation

XO type of sex determination can be found in :

- **(1)** Grasshoppers
- (2) Monkeys
- (3) Drosophila
- (4) Birds

**Answer** — booklet: (1) · independent solve: (1) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Grasshopper is an example of XO type of sex determination in which the males have only one X-chromosome besides the autosomes, whereas females have a pair of X-chromosomes.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. In XO sex determination males carry a single X chromosome and no Y ($XO$), while females are $XX$; this is seen in grasshoppers, so the answer is option $1$ (Drosophila and monkeys are $XY$, birds are $ZW$).

## Q117

Biology · botany · Transport in Plants

“Girdling Experiment” was performed by Plant Physiologists to identify the plant tissue through which:

- (1) for both water and food transportation
- (2) osmosis is observed
- (3) water is transported
- **(4)** food is transported

**Answer** — booklet: (4) · independent solve: (4) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. The girdling experiment shows that phloem is the tissue responsible for translocation of food; and that transport takes place in one direction $i.e.$ towards the root.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Girdling removes a ring of bark including phloem but leaves xylem intact; food accumulates above the ring and tissue below starves, showing phloem transports food (option $4$).

## Q119

Biology · botany · Biotechnology: Principles and Processes

Which one of the following statement is **not true** regarding gel electrophoresis technique?

- **(1)** The presence of chromogenic substrate gives blue coloured DNA bands on the gel.
- (2) Bright orange coloured bands of DNA can be observed in the gel when exposed to UV light.
- (3) The process of extraction of separated DNA strands from gel is called elution.
- (4) The separated DNA fragments are stained by using ethidium bromide.

**Answer** — booklet: (1) · independent solve: (1) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Option (1) is the incorrect statement, as bright colored bands of DNA can be observed in the gel when EtBr (Ethidium bromide) treated DNA is exposed to UV light.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Elution (3), ethidium bromide staining (4) and bright orange bands under UV light (2) are all true of gel electrophoresis; a chromogenic substrate giving blue colour relates to blue-white selection of recombinant colonies, not to DNA bands on a gel, so statement $3$ is not true.

## Q120

Biology · botany · Ecosystem

Given below are two statements:
In the light of the above statements, choose the **correct** answer from the options given below:

- (Statement I) Decomposition is a process in which the detritus is degraded into simpler substances by microbes.
- (Statement II) Decomposition is faster if the detritus is rich in lignin and chitin.

- **(1)** **Statement I** is correct but **Statement II** is incorrect
- (2) **Statement I** is incorrect but **Statement II** is correct
- (3) Both **Statement I** and **Statement II** are correct
- (4) Both **Statement I** and **Statement II** are incorrect

**Answer** — booklet: (1) · independent solve: (1) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Decomposition is the process by which decomposers breakdown complex organic matter into inorganic substances.
2. The rate of decomposition is controlled by chemical composition of detritus and climatic factors. Decomposition is slower if detritus is rich in lignin and chitin and quicker, if detritus is rich in nitrogen and water soluble substances like sugars.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Statement I: decomposers (microbes) break down detritus into simpler inorganic substances, so Statement I is correct.
2. Statement II: decomposition is slower, not faster, when detritus is rich in lignin and chitin, so Statement II is incorrect.
3. Statement I correct and Statement II incorrect, which is option $1$.

**Subtopic:** Decomposition · **Difficulty:** medium (3 steps)

## Q121

Biology · botany · Plant Growth and Development

Which one of the following plants does **not** show plasticity?

- (1) Buttercup
- **(2)** Maize
- (3) Cotton
- (4) Coriander

**Answer** — booklet: (2) · independent solve: (2) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Plants follow different pathways in response to environment or phases of life to form different kinds of structures. This ability is called plasticity e.g. heterophylly in cotton, coriander and larkspur. In such plants, leaves of juvenile plant are different in a shape from those in mature plants.
2. Maize does not show plasticity.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Plasticity (heterophylly) is shown by cotton, coriander, larkspur and buttercup per NCERT, while maize shows no heterophylly, so the answer is option $2$.

## Q123

Biology · botany · Sexual Reproduction in Flowering Plants

Given below are two statements :
In the light of the above statements, choose the **correct** answer from the options given below :

- (Statement I) Cleistogamous flowers are invariably autogamous
- (Statement II) Cleistogamy is disadvantageous as there is no chance for cross pollination

- (1) Statement I is correct but Statement II is incorrect
- (2) Statement I is incorrect but Statement II is correct
- **(3)** Both Statement I and Statement II are correct
- (4) Both Statement I and Statement II are incorrect

**Answer** — booklet: (3) · independent solve: (3) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Cleistogamous flowers does not open at all. In such flowers autogamy occurs.
2. Lack of cross pollination is a disadvantage of cleistogamy.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Cleistogamous flowers do not open at all, so anthers and stigma lie close together inside the closed flower.
2. Pollen can reach only the stigma of the same flower, so cleistogamous flowers are invariably autogamous: Statement I is correct.
3. Because the flower never opens, there is no chance of cross-pollination, so no new genetic recombination; this is a disadvantage: Statement II is correct.
4. Both statements correct $\Rightarrow$ option $3$.

**Subtopic:** Pollination · **Difficulty:** medium (3 steps)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 47)

![printed question 123](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/04112026/neet_papers/2022/2022_S1/crops/q123_37029cd0_04112026.png?v=37029cd0a434)

## Q127

Biology · botany · Anatomy of Flowering Plants

> ⚠ The booklet prints "Answer (NA) No option is correct": none of the four options is correct as printed, so there is no keyed answer to check a solve against.

> ⚠ The printed solution says "All the statements are correct regarding vascular bundles but none of the options with such combination is given.": the question has no correct option as printed, and the printed answer comes from a corrected reading.

> ⚠ The independent solve found no printed option exactly right ("No option lists exactly the set of true statements: by NCERT all five statements (a)-(e) are true, but every option omits at least one"); its pick (unsure) is the closest fit, so treat this question as possibly misprinted.

Read the following statements about the vascular bundles :

- (a) In roots, xylem and phloem in a vascular bundle are arranged in an alternate manner along the different radii.
- (b) Conjoint closed vascular bundles do not possess cambium
- (c) In open vascular bundles, cambium is present in between xylem and phloem
- (d) The vascular bundles of dicotyledonous stem possess endarch protoxylem
- (e) In monocotyledonous root, usually there are more than six xylem bundles present

- (1) (a), (b), (c) and (d) Only
- (2) (a), (c), (d) and (e) Only
- (3) (a), (b) and (d) Only
- (4) (b), (c), (d) and (e) Only

**Answer** — booklet: (none) · independent solve: (unsure) · publisher_no_valid_option

**Publisher's solution**

1. All the statements are correct regarding vascular bundles but none of the options with such combination is given.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. (a) In roots xylem and phloem lie on alternate radii (radial bundles): true.
2. (b) Conjoint closed bundles have no cambium: true.
3. (c) In open bundles cambium lies between xylem and phloem: true.
4. (d) Dicot stem vascular bundles have endarch protoxylem: true.
5. (e) Monocot root is polyarch, usually more than six xylem bundles: true.
6. True set $= \{a, b, c, d, e\}$; options give $\{a,b,d\}$, $\{b,c,d,e\}$, $\{a,b,c,d\}$, $\{a,c,d,e\}$; none matches exactly.

## Q128

Biology · botany · Plant Kingdom

Which of the following is **incorrectly** matched?

- (1) *Porphyra* – Floridian Starch
- (2) *Volvox* – Starch
- (3) *Ectocarpus* – Fucoxanthin
- **(4)** *Ulothrix* – Mannitol

**Answer** — booklet: (4) · independent solve: (4) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. $\textit{Ulothrix}$ is a member of Chlorophyceae (green algae), with reserve food material, starch.
2. Mannitol is stored food material of Phaeophyceae (brown algae).

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. $\text{Ectocarpus}$ is a brown alga (Phaeophyceae) whose characteristic pigment is fucoxanthin, so option $3$ is correctly matched.
2. $\text{Ulothrix}$ is a green alga (Chlorophyceae) whose reserve food is starch; mannitol is stored by brown algae, so option $4$ is incorrectly matched.
3. $\text{Porphyra}$ is a red alga (Rhodophyceae) storing floridean starch, so option $1$ is correctly matched.
4. $\text{Volvox}$ is a green alga storing starch, so option $2$ is correctly matched.
5. The only incorrect pair is option $4$.

**Subtopic:** Chlorophyceae, Phaeophyceae · **Difficulty:** medium (2 steps)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 48)

## Q129

Biology · botany · Biotechnology: Principles and Processes

Given below are two statements : one is labelled as Assertion (A) and the other is labelled as Reason (R). In the light of the above statements, choose the correct answer from the options given below :

- Assertion (A) Polymerase chain reaction is used in DNA amplification.
- Reason (R) The ampicillin resistant gene is used as a selectable marker to check transformation

- (1) (A) is correct but (R) is not correct
- (2) (A) is not correct but (R) is correct
- (3) Both (A) and (R) are correct and (R) is the correct explanation of (A)
- **(4)** Both (A) and (R) are correct but (R) is not the correct explanation of (A)

**Answer** — booklet: (4) · independent solve: (4) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Option (4) is the correct answer because both the statements are correct but the given reason is not the correct explanation. Polymerase chain reaction is used in DNA amplification.
2. Ampicillin resistance gene is a selectable marker that helps to check transformation by selection of transformants.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Polymerase chain reaction makes many copies of a target DNA segment, so (A) is correct.
2. The ampicillin resistance gene (e.g. $amp^R$ in pBR322) is used as a selectable marker to identify transformants, so (R) is correct.
3. Selection of transformants by antibiotic resistance has no bearing on how PCR amplifies DNA, so (R) is not the explanation of (A): option $4$.

**Subtopic:** Amplification of Gene of Interest Using PCR, ORI and Selectable Marker · **Difficulty:** medium (3 steps)

## Q130

Biology · botany · Respiration in Plants

What is the net gain of ATP when each molecule of glucose is converted to two molecules of pyruvic acid?

- **(1)** Two
- (2) Eight
- (3) Four
- (4) Six

**Answer** — booklet: (1) · independent solve: (1) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. During glycolysis, total $4$ ATPs are produced from one glucose molecule with a net gain of $2$ ATPs.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Glycolysis converts one glucose into two pyruvic acid molecules and uses $2$ ATP (hexokinase and phosphofructokinase steps).
2. It forms $4$ ATP by substrate-level phosphorylation ($2$ per triose).
3. Net gain $= 4 - 2 = 2$ ATP, which is option $1$ (Two).

**Subtopic:** Glycolysis · **Difficulty:** medium (2 steps)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 49)

## Q131

Biology · botany · Plant Growth and Development

The gaseous plant growth regulator is used in plants to :

- (1) help overcome apical dominance
- (2) kill dicotyledonous weeds in the fields
- (3) speed up the malting process
- **(4)** promote root growth and roothair formation to increase the absorption surface

**Answer** — booklet: (4) · independent solve: (4) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Ethylene is a gaseous plant hormone. It induces development of adventitious roots on various types of cutting. It promotes the development of lateral roots and growth of root hairs. Cytokinin helps to overcome the apical dominance.
2. Auxin is used to kill dicot weeds. Gibberellin speeds up the malting process

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. The only gaseous plant growth regulator is ethylene; per NCERT it promotes root growth and root hair formation to increase the absorption surface, while options 3, 1 and 2 describe gibberellins, cytokinins and auxin (2,4-D) respectively, so the answer is option $4$.

**Subtopic:** Ethylene and Abscisic Acid · **Difficulty:** medium (2 steps)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 49)

## Q132

Biology · botany · Photosynthesis in Higher Plants

Given below are two statements : In the light of the above statements, choose the **correct** answer from the options given below:

- (Statement I) The primary $\mathrm{CO_2}$ acceptor in $\mathrm{C_4}$ plants is phosphoenolpyruvate and is found in the mesophyll cells.
- (Statement II) Mesophyll cells of $\mathrm{C_4}$ plants lack RuBisCo enzyme.

- (1) Statement I is correct but Statement II is incorrect
- (2) Statement I is incorrect but Statement II is correct
- **(3)** Both Statement I and Statement II are correct
- (4) Both Statement I and Statement II are incorrect

**Answer** — booklet: (3) · independent solve: (3) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. The primary $\mathrm{CO_2}$ acceptor is a 3-carbon molecule, phosphoenol pyruvate (PEP) and is present in the mesophyll cells.
2. Mesophyll cells of $\mathrm{C_4}$ plants lack RuBisCO enzyme.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Statement I: in $\mathrm{C_4}$ plants the primary $\mathrm{CO_2}$ acceptor is phosphoenolpyruvate (PEP), a 3-carbon molecule found in the mesophyll cells, so it is correct.
2. Statement II: the mesophyll cells of $\mathrm{C_4}$ plants lack RuBisCO, which is present in the bundle sheath cells, so it is correct.
3. Both statements are correct, which is option $3$.

## Q133

Biology · botany · Biodiversity and Conservation

Which of the following is **not** a method of *ex situ* conservation?

- (1) Micropropagation
- (2) Cryopreservation
- (3) *In vitro* fertilization
- **(4)** National Parks

**Answer** — booklet: (4) · independent solve: (4) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. *In-situ* conservation means on site conservation i.e. when we conserve and protect the whole ecosystem, its biodiversity at all levels is protected.
2. National parks are type of *in-situ* conservation.
3. Whereas, micropropagation, cryopreservation and *in-vitro* fertilisation are methods of *ex-situ* conservation.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. National Parks conserve organisms in their natural habitat, which is in situ conservation, whereas in vitro fertilization, micropropagation and cryopreservation are ex situ methods, so the answer is option $4$.

**Subtopic:** Ex Situ Conservation, In Situ Conservation · **Difficulty:** easy (1 step)

## Q134

Biology · botany · Photosynthesis in Higher Plants

Which one of the following is **not** true regarding the release of energy during ATP synthesis through chemiosmosis? It involves:

- (1) Movement of protons across the membrane to the stroma
- (2) Reduction of NADP to $\mathrm{NADPH_2}$ on the stroma side of the membrane
- (3) Breakdown of proton gradient
- **(4)** Breakdown of electron gradient

**Answer** — booklet: (4) · independent solve: (4) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Chemiosmosis requires a membrane, a proton pump, a proton gradient and ATP synthase. Energy is used to pump protons across a membrane to create a gradient or a high concentration of protons within the thylakoid lumen.
2. The NADP reductase enzyme is located on the stroma side of the membrane. Along with the electrons that come from the acceptor of electrons of PS I, protons are necessary for reduction of $\mathrm{NADP^{+}}$ to $\mathrm{NADPH + H^{+}}$.
3. The process does not involve breaking of electron gradient.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. In chemiosmosis, a proton gradient forms across the thylakoid membrane; its breakdown, as protons move through CF$_0$ of ATP synthase to the stroma, releases energy for ATP synthesis, so options $3$ and $1$ are true; NADP reductase on the stroma side reduces NADP to NADPH, so option $2$ is true; no electron gradient exists, so option $4$ is not true.

**Subtopic:** Chemiosmotic Hypothesis · **Difficulty:** medium (2 steps)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 50)

![printed question 134](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/04112026/neet_papers/2022/2022_S1/crops/q134_0a47360b_04112026.png?v=0a47360bad09)

## Q135

Biology · botany · Cell Cycle and Cell Division

Which one of the following never occurs during mitotic cell division?

- **(1)** Pairing of homologous chromosomes
- (2) Coiling and condensation of the chromatids
- (3) Spindle fibres attach to kinetochores of chromosomes
- (4) Movement of centrioles towards opposite poles

**Answer** — booklet: (1) · independent solve: (1) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Pairing of homologous chromosomes occurs during prophase I of meiosis.
2. Coiling and condensation of chromatids, spindle fibres attachment to the kinetochores and movement of centrioles towards opposite poles occur in both mitosis and meiosis.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Homologous chromosome pairing (synapsis) happens only in zygotene of meiosis I, so it never occurs in mitosis; options 3, 4 and 2 all occur in mitosis, so the answer is option $1$.

**Subtopic:** Prophase and Metaphase, Prophase I and Its Types · **Difficulty:** easy (1 step)

## Q136

Biology · botany · Principles of Inheritance and Variation

Given below are two statements : one is labelled as **Assertion (A)** and the other is labelled as **Reason (R)**.

In the light of the above statements, choose the **correct** answer from the options given below:

- Assertion (A) Mendel's law of Independent assortment does not hold good for the genes that are located closely on the same chromosome.
- Reason (R) Closely located genes assort independently.

- **(1)** (A) is correct but (R) is not correct
- (2) (A) is not correct but (R) is correct
- (3) Both (A) and (R) are correct and (R) is the correct explanation of (A)
- (4) Both (A) and (R) are correct but (R) is not the correct explanation of (A)

**Answer** — booklet: (1) · independent solve: (1) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Closely located genes do not show independent assortment. Mendel's law of independent assortment holds good for those genes which are located on different chromosomes.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Genes located close together on the same chromosome are linked and are usually inherited together, so Mendel's law of independent assortment does not apply to them: (A) is correct.
2. (R) says closely located genes assort independently, which contradicts linkage, so (R) is not correct; this is option $1$.

**Subtopic:** Linkage & Recombination · **Difficulty:** medium (2 steps)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 50)

- (2) It cannot be adulterated like diesel

NCERT lists CNG advantages: it burns most efficiently, is cheaper than petrol or diesel, and cannot be siphoned off or adulterated, so options $3$, $1$ and $2$ are true; the statement that the same diesel engine is used with low conversion cost is not an NCERT advantage and is false, so the answer is option $4$.

## Q139

Biology · botany · Biomolecules

Read the following statements on lipids and find out correct set of statements:

- (a) Lecithin found in the plasma membrane is a glycolipid
- (b) Saturated fatty acids possess one or more $c = c$ bonds
- (c) Gingely oil has lower melting point, hence remains as oil in winter
- (d) Lipids are generally insoluble in water but soluble in some organic solvents
- (e) When fatty acid is esterified with glycerol, monoglycerides are formed

- **(1)** (c), (d) and (e) only
- (2) (a), (b) and (d) only
- (3) (a), (b) and (c) only
- (4) (a), (d) and (e) only

**Answer** — booklet: (1) · independent solve: (1) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Option (1) is the correct answer because statements (c), (d) and (e) are correct as oils have lower melting point and hence remain oil in winters. Lipids are generally insoluble in water but soluble in some organic solvents.
2. Option (2), (3) and (4) are incorrect because statements (a) and (b) are incorrect. Lecithin is a type of phospholipid found in plasma membrane. Saturated fatty acids are without double bond.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Lecithin found in plasma membrane is a phospholipid, not a glycolipid, so (a) is false.
2. Saturated fatty acids by definition lack C=C double bonds (they are 'saturated' with hydrogen), so (b) is false.
3. Gingely (sesame) oil has a lower melting point and remains liquid even in winter, so (c) is true.
4. Lipids are generally insoluble in water but soluble in organic solvents like ether/chloroform, so (d) is true.
5. Esterification of one fatty acid molecule with glycerol produces a monoglyceride, so (e) is true.
6. True statements are (c), (d), (e), matching option 1.

**Subtopic:** Lipids · **Difficulty:** hard (6 steps)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 51)

![printed question 139](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/04112026/neet_papers/2022/2022_S1/crops/q139_b919be72_04112026.png?v=b919be72d02e)

Publisher's solution, as printed:

## Q140

Biology · botany · Biotechnology and Its Applications

Transposons can be used during which one of the following ?

- (1) Autoradiography
- (2) Gene sequencing
- (3) Polymerase Chain Reaction
- **(4)** Gene Silencing

**Answer** — booklet: (4) · independent solve: (4) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Option (4) is the correct answer as the source of the complementary RNA for RNAi could be mobile genetic elements (transposons) that replicate via an RNA intermediate.
2. Option (1) is incorrect as autoradiography usally follows hybridisation.
3. Option (3) is incorrect because polymerase chain reaction is used to make copies of the DNA sample and does not need transposons.
4. Option (2) is incorrect because transposons are not required during gene sequencing.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. RNAi (gene silencing) uses complementary dsRNA from viruses with RNA genomes or from transposons replicating via an RNA intermediate, so the answer is option $4$, Gene Silencing.

**Subtopic:** Pest Resistant Plants and Bt Cotton · **Difficulty:** medium (1 step)

## Q141

Biology · botany · Anatomy of Flowering Plants

The anatomy of springwood shows some peculiar features. Identify the **correct** set of statements about springwood.

- (a) It is also called as the earlywood
- (b) In spring season cambium produces xylem elements with narrow vessels
- (c) It is lighter in colour
- (d) The springwood along with autumnwood shows alternate concentric rings forming annual rings
- (e) It has lower density

- (1) (a), (b) and (d) Only
- (2) (c), (d) and (e) Only
- (3) (a), (b), (d) and (e) Only
- **(4)** (a), (c), (d) and (e) Only

**Answer** — booklet: (4) · independent solve: (4) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Spring wood is also called early wood. It is lighter in colour and has a lower density. The vessels are produced with the wider lumens to transport more water to meet the requirement by increased transpiring surface in spring season.
2. The spring and autumn wood appear as alternate concentric rings of light and dark colour forming annual rings.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. (a) Springwood is also called earlywood: true.
2. (b) In spring the cambium is very active and forms xylem with wider vessels, not narrow: false.
3. (c) Springwood is lighter in colour: true.
4. (d) Springwood and autumnwood appear as alternate concentric rings forming annual rings: true.
5. (e) Springwood has lower density: true.
6. True set is $(a), (c), (d), (e)$, which is option $4$.

**Subtopic:** Secondary Growth · **Difficulty:** hard (6 steps)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 52)

## Q142

Biology · botany · Organisms and Populations

While explaining interspecific interaction of population, (+) sign is assigned for beneficial interaction, (–) sign is assigned for detrimental interaction and (0) for neutral interaction. - (1) Commensalism
- (2) Competition
- **(3)** Predation
- (4) Amensalim

**Answer** — booklet: (3) · independent solve: (3) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. In predation, one species is benefitted where as the other is harmed. It is $(+\;-)$ type of population interaction.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Predation is $(+,-)$, amensalism $(-,0)$, commensalism $(+,0)$ and competition $(-,-)$, so the $(+,-)$ interaction is predation, option $3$.

## Q143

Which of the following occurs due to the presence of autosome linked dominant trait ?

- (2) Thalessemia
- (3) Sickle cell anaemia
- **(4)** Myotonic dystrophy

## Q145

Biology · botany · Biotechnology: Principles and Processes

In the following palindromic base sequences of DNA, which one can be cut easily by particular restriction enzyme?

- (1) $5'$CTCAGT$3'$; $3'$GAGTCA$5'$
- (2) $5'$GTATTC$3'$; $3'$CATAAG$5'$
- (3) $5'$GATACT$3'$; $3'$CTATGA$5'$
- **(4)** $5'$GAATTC$3'$; $3'$CTTAAG$5'$

**Answer** — booklet: (4) · independent solve: (4) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Option (4) is the correct answer as a palindromic DNA sequence is a DNA sequence of base pairs that reads same on the two strands when orientation of reading is kept the same. Out of the four options, option (4) is the only palindromic sequence.
2. $5'$GAATTC$3'$
3. $3'$CTTAAG$5'$

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. A restriction enzyme recognises a palindromic sequence, one that reads the same on both strands in the $5' \to 3'$ direction.
2. Option 4: top $5'\mathrm{GAATTC}3'$; bottom read $5' \to 3'$ is $\mathrm{GAATTC}$, so it is palindromic and is the EcoRI recognition site.
3. Options 3, 1, 2: bottom strands read $5' \to 3'$ are $\mathrm{AGTATC}$, $\mathrm{TGACTC}$, $\mathrm{GAATAC}$, which differ from the top strands, so they are not palindromes.
4. Hence the answer is option $4$.

**Subtopic:** Restriction Enzymes and Their Types · **Difficulty:** medium (2 steps)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 53)

## Q146

Biology · botany · Photosynthesis in Higher Plants

What is the role of large bundle sheath cells found around the vascular bundles in $\mathrm{C_4}$ plants?

- (1) To enable the plant to tolerate high temperature
- (2) To protect the vascular tissue from high light intensity
- (3) To provide the site for photorespiratory pathway
- **(4)** To increase the number of chloroplast for the operation of Calvin cycle

**Answer** — booklet: (4) · independent solve: (4) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. The large cells around the vascular bundles of $\mathrm{C_4}$ plants form bundle sheath. These cells have large number of chloroplasts to perform calvin cycle.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. In $\mathrm{C_4}$ plants (Kranz anatomy), the large bundle sheath cells hold many chloroplasts and carry out the Calvin cycle, while photorespiration is suppressed, so option $4$ fits.

**Subtopic:** The C₄ Pathway · **Difficulty:** medium (1 step)

## Q147

Biology · botany · Molecular Basis of Inheritance

If a geneticist uses the blind approach for sequencing the whole genome of an organism, followed by assignment of function to different segments, the methodology adopted by him is called as :

- (1) Expressed sequence tags
- (2) Bioinformatics
- **(3)** Sequence annotation
- (4) Gene mapping

**Answer** — booklet: (3) · independent solve: (3) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Sequencing the whole set of genome that contained all the coding and non-coding sequences and later assigning different regions in the sequence with fuctions is called sequence annotation.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. NCERT (HGP) describes two approaches: identifying expressed genes as RNA (Expressed Sequence Tags), and the blind approach of sequencing the whole genome and later assigning functions to different regions, termed Sequence Annotation, so option $3$.

**Subtopic:** Human Genome Project · **Difficulty:** medium (1 step)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 53)

## Q148

Biology · botany · Cell: The Unit of Life

Match List-I with List-II.

Choose the **correct answer** from the options given below :

| List-I | | List-II | |
|---|---|---|---|
| (a) | Metacentric chromosome | (i) | Centromere situated close to the end forming one extremely short and one very long arms |
| (b) | Acrocentric chromosome | (ii) | Centromere at the terminal end |
| (c) | Submetacentric | (iii) | Centromere in the middle forming two equal arms of chromosomes |
| (d) | Telocentric chromosome | (iv) | Centromere slightly away from the middle forming one shorter arm and one longer arm |

- (1) (a)-(ii), (b)-(iii), (c)-(iv), (d)-(i)
- (2) (a)-(i), (b)-(ii), (c)-(iii), (d)-(iv)
- **(3)** (a)-(iii), (b)-(i), (c)-(iv), (d)-(ii)
- (4) (a)-(i), (b)-(iii), (c)-(ii), (d)-(iv)

**Answer** — booklet: (3) · independent solve: (3) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. In metacentric chromosome, centromere is in the middle of the chromosomes.
2. Acrocentric chromosome has centromere close to the end of the chromosome.
3. In submetacentric chromosome, centromere is slightly away from the middle of the chromosome.
4. Telocentric chromosome has terminal centromere.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Metacentric chromosome: centromere in the middle forming two equal arms, so $(a) \to (iii)$.
2. Acrocentric chromosome: centromere close to the end giving one extremely short and one very long arm, so $(b) \to (i)$.
3. Submetacentric: centromere slightly away from the middle giving one shorter and one longer arm, so $(c) \to (iv)$.
4. Telocentric chromosome: centromere at the terminal end, so $(d) \to (ii)$.
5. The set $(a)\text{-}(iii), (b)\text{-}(i), (c)\text{-}(iv), (d)\text{-}(ii)$ is option $3$.

**Subtopic:** Nucleus, Chromosome And Microbodies · **Difficulty:** easy (1 step)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 54)

![printed question 148](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/04112026/neet_papers/2022/2022_S1/crops/q148_fc173e1a_04112026.png?v=fc173e1a600c)

## Q149

Biology · botany · Transport in Plants

Addition of more solutes in a given solution will :

- (1) make its water potential zero
- (2) not affect the water potential at all
- (3) raise its water potential
- **(4)** lower its water potential

**Answer** — booklet: (4) · independent solve: (4) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. If some solute is dissolved in pure water, the solution has lower free water and the concentration of water decreases, reducing it's water potential.
2. The magnitude of this lowering due to dissolution of a solute is called solute potential.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Water potential is $\Psi_w = \Psi_s + \Psi_p$; adding more solute makes $\Psi_s$ more negative, so $\Psi_w$ is lowered, which is option $4$.

**Subtopic:** Water Potential · **Difficulty:** easy (1 step)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 54)

![printed question 149](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/04112026/neet_papers/2022/2022_S1/crops/q149_2735a6a9_04112026.png?v=2735a6a991cb)

## Q150

Biology · botany · Plant Kingdom

Match the plant with the kind of life cycle it exhibits:

Choose the **correct answer** from the options given below :

| List-I | | List-II | |
|---|---|---|---|
| (a) | Spirogyra | (i) | Dominant diploid sporophyte vascular plant, with highly reduced male or female gametophyte |
| (b) | Fern | (ii) | Dominant haploid free-living gametophyte |
| (c) | Funaria | (iii) | Dominant diploid sporophyte alternating with reduced gametophyte called prothallus |
| (d) | Cycas | (iv) | Dominant haploid leafy gametophyte alternating with partially dependent multicellular sporophyte |

- (1) (a)-(iii), (b)-(iv), (c)-(i), (d)-(ii)
- (2) (a)-(ii), (b)-(iv), (c)-(i), (d)-(iii)
- (3) (a)-(iv), (b)-(i), (c)-(ii), (d)-(iii)
- **(4)** (a)-(ii), (b)-(iii), (c)-(iv), (d)-(i)

**Answer** — booklet: (4) · independent solve: (4) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. $\textit{Spirogyra}$ is an alga. It shows haplontic life-cycle.
2. Fern is pteridophyte. The dominant phase of life-cycle is diploid sporophyte. Its gametophyte is called prothallus.
3. $\textit{Funaria}$ is a bryophyte. Its gametophyte is a leafy stage.
4. $\textit{Cycas}$ is a gymnosperm. The main plant body in gymnosperm is sporophyte. They have highly reduced gametophyte stage.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Spirogyra (alga) is haplontic: dominant haploid free-living gametophyte, so $(a)-(ii)$.
2. Fern (pteridophyte): dominant diploid sporophyte alternating with a reduced gametophyte called prothallus, so $(b)-(iii)$.
3. Funaria (moss): dominant haploid leafy gametophyte with a partially dependent multicellular sporophyte, so $(c)-(iv)$.
4. Cycas (gymnosperm): dominant diploid vascular sporophyte with highly reduced male or female gametophyte, so $(d)-(i)$.
5. The match $(a)-(ii), (b)-(iii), (c)-(iv), (d)-(i)$ is option $4$.

**Subtopic:** Plant Life Cycles and Alternation of Generations · **Difficulty:** medium (3 steps)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 54)

![printed question 150](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/04112026/neet_papers/2022/2022_S1/crops/q150_136b1793_04112026.png?v=136b1793f348)

## Q151

Biology · zoology · Breathing and Exchange of Gases

Which of the following is not the function of conducting part of respiratory system?

- (1) Temperature of inhaled air is brought to body temperature
- **(2)** Provides surface for diffusion of $\mathrm{O_2}$ and $\mathrm{CO_2}$
- (3) It clears inhaled air from foreign particles
- (4) Inhaled air is humidified

**Answer** — booklet: (2) · independent solve: (2) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Option (2) is correct because the part starting with the external nostrils upto the terminal bronchioles constitute the conducting part; whereas the alveoli and their ducts form the respiratory or exchange part of the respiratory system.
2. The conducting part transports the atmospheric air to the alveoli, clears it from foreign particles, humidifies and also bring the air to body temperature. Exchange part is the site of actual diffusion of $\mathrm{O_2}$/$\mathrm{CO_2}$ between blood and atmospheric air.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. The conducting part (nose to terminal bronchioles) clears foreign particles from inhaled air, humidifies it and brings it to body temperature, while diffusion of $\mathrm{O_2}$ and $\mathrm{CO_2}$ happens across the alveoli of the exchange part, so the answer is option $2$.

**Subtopic:** Human Respiratory System · **Difficulty:** easy (1 step)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 55)

![printed question 151](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/04112026/neet_papers/2022/2022_S1/crops/q151_0240cc01_04112026.png?v=0240cc013dec)

## Q152

Biology · zoology · Biomolecules

A dehydration reaction links two glucose molecules to product maltose. If the formula for glucose is $\mathrm{C_6H_{12}O_6}$ then what is the formula for maltose?

- **(1)** $\mathrm{C_{12}H_{22}O_{11}}$
- (2) $\mathrm{C_{12}H_{24}O_{11}}$
- (3) $\mathrm{C_{12}H_{20}O_{10}}$
- (4) $\mathrm{C_{12}H_{24}O_{12}}$

**Answer** — booklet: (1) · independent solve: (1) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Option (1) is correct because maltose is a disaccharide formed by dehydration process $i.e.$, synthesis by elimination of one water molecule to form a glycosidic bond in between two glucose molecules. So, its molecular formula is.
2. $\mathrm{C_6H_{12}O_6} \times 2 \xrightarrow[\mathrm{H_2O}]{} \mathrm{C_{12}H_{22}O_{11}}$

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Two glucose molecules together contain $2 \times \mathrm{C_6H_{12}O_6} = \mathrm{C_{12}H_{24}O_{12}}$.
2. A dehydration (condensation) reaction forming one glycosidic bond removes one $\mathrm{H_2O}$.
3. $\mathrm{C_{12}H_{24}O_{12}} - \mathrm{H_2O} = \mathrm{C_{12}H_{22}O_{11}}$, which is option $1$.

**Subtopic:** Polysaccharides · **Difficulty:** easy (1 step)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 55)

![printed question 152](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/04112026/neet_papers/2022/2022_S1/crops/q152_cab621d8_04112026.png?v=cab621d853ca)

Publisher's solution, as printed:

![printed solution 152](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/04112026/neet_papers/2022/2022_S1/solutions/q152_2da5517a_04112026.png?v=2da5517ab728)

## Q153

Biology · zoology · Biotechnology: Principles and Processes

Given below are two statements:

In the light of the above statements, choose the **most appropriate** answer from the options given below:

- (Statement I) Restriction endonucleases recognise specific sequence to cut DNA known as palindromic nucleotide sequence.
- (Statement II) Restriction endonucleases cut the DNA strand a little away from the centre of the palindromic site.

- (1) Statement I is correct but Statement II is incorrect
- (2) Statement I is incorrect but Statement II is correct
- **(3)** Both Statement I and Statement II are correct
- (4) Both Statement I and Statement II are incorrect

**Answer** — booklet: (3) · independent solve: (3) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Option (3) is the correct answer because both the statements I and II are correct.
2. Each restriction endonuclease recognises a specific palindromic nucleotide sequences in the DNA. It will bind to the DNA and cut each of the two strands of double helix at specific points.
3. Restriction enzymes cut the strand of DNA a little away from the centre of the palindrome site; but between the same two bases on the opposite strands. So both the statements I and II are correct.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Statement I: Restriction endonucleases recognise a specific palindromic nucleotide sequence — this matches NCERT's description of restriction enzyme action, so it is correct.
2. Statement II: NCERT explicitly states that the enzyme cuts each strand at a point a little away from the centre of the palindromic site (between the same two bases on opposite strands), generating sticky ends — so this is also correct.
3. Since both statements are correct, option 3 is the answer.

**Subtopic:** Restriction Enzymes and Their Types · **Difficulty:** medium (3 steps)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 55)

![printed question 153](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/04112026/neet_papers/2022/2022_S1/crops/q153_f31ca04f_04112026.png?v=f31ca04fcf07)

## Q154

Biology · zoology · Locomotion and Movement

Given below are two statements : one is labelled as **Assertion (A)** and the other is labelled as **Reason (R)**.
In the light of the above statements, choose the **most appropriate** answer from the options given below.

- Assertion (A) Osteoporosis is characterised by decreased bone mass and increased chance of fractures.
- Reason (R) Common cause of osteoporosis is increased levels of estrogen.

- **(1)** **(A)** is correct but **(R)** is not correct
- (2) **(A)** is not correct but **(R)** is correct
- (3) Both **(A)** and **(R)** are correct and **(R)** is the correct explanation of **(A)**
- (4) Both **(A)** and **(R)** are correct but **(R)** is not the correct explanation of **(A)**

**Answer** — booklet: (1) · independent solve: (1) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Option (1) is the correct answer as osteoporosis is due to decreased levels of oestrogen.
2. Osteoporosis is an age-related disorder characterised by decreased bone mass hence, the chances of fractures increase.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Osteoporosis: decreased bone mass, increased fracture risk - A is correct.
2. Osteoporosis is caused by decreased estrogen (e.g., post-menopause), not increased estrogen - R is incorrect.
3. Therefore A is correct but R is not correct, matching option 1.

**Subtopic:** Disorders of Muscular and Skeletal System · **Difficulty:** medium (2 steps)

## Q155

Biology · zoology · Molecular Basis of Inheritance

In an $\textit{E. Coli}$ strain $i$ gene gets mutated and its product can not bind the inducer molecule. If growth medium is provided with lactose, what will be the outcome?

- **(1)** $z$, $y$, $a$ genes will not be translated
- (2) RNA polymerase will bind the promoter region
- (3) Only $z$ gene will get transcribed
- (4) $z$, $y$, $a$ genes will be transcribed

**Answer** — booklet: (1) · independent solve: (1) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. As the product of '$i$' gene binds with the operator region and blocks the transcription and translation of $z$, $y$ and $a$ genes.
2. It's product is prevented from binding to the operator by attaching it with the inducer. As the inducer can now no more capable of binding with the repressor, thus, in all the cases, operator always gets attached with the repressor thereby preventing the transcription and transmission of $z$, $y$ and $a$.
3. Even in the presence of lactose, transcription and translation of $z$, $y$ and $a$ would not occur.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. The $i$ gene codes for the repressor; normally the inducer (allolactose) binds the repressor and releases it from the operator.
2. Here the mutated repressor cannot bind the inducer, so even with lactose present it stays bound to the operator.
3. The bound repressor blocks RNA polymerase, so $z$, $y$, $a$ are not transcribed and therefore not translated, which is option $1$.

**Subtopic:** The Lac Operon · **Difficulty:** hard (4 steps)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 56)

![printed question 155](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/04112026/neet_papers/2022/2022_S1/crops/q155_619bf77b_04112026.png?v=619bf77bdad0)

Publisher's solution, as printed:

![printed solution 155](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/04112026/neet_papers/2022/2022_S1/solutions/q155_a3e70341_04112026.png?v=a3e70341344e)

## Q156

Biology · zoology · Structural Organisation in Animals

Tegmina in cockroach, arises from

- (1) Metathorax
- (2) Prothorax and Mesothorax
- (3) Prothorax
- **(4)** Mesothorax

**Answer** — booklet: (4) · independent solve: (4) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Option (4) is the correct answer because tegmina or forewings (the first pair of wings) in cockroach arises from mesothorax.
2. Options (1), (2) and (3) are incorrect because no wing arises from prothorax and hindwings arise from metathorax.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Tegmina (the opaque, leathery forewings of cockroach) arise from the mesothorax, while the hindwings arise from the metathorax, so the answer is option $4$.

## Q157

Biology · zoology · Human Health and Disease

Given below are two statements:
Statement I:
Statement II:
In the light of the above statements, choose the most appropriate answer from the options given below:

- (Statement I) Autoimmune disorder is a condition where body defense mechanism recognizes its own cells as foreign bodies.
- (Statement II) Rheumatoid arthritis is a condition where body does not attack self cells.

- **(1)** Statement I is correct but Statement II is incorrect
- (2) Statement I is incorrect but Statement II is correct
- (3) Both Statement I and Statement II are correct
- (4) Both Statement I and Statement II are incorrect

**Answer** — booklet: (1) · independent solve: (1) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Option (1) is the correct answer as autoimmune disorder is a condition where body defense mechanism recognises its own cells as foreign bodies. Sometimes, due to genetic and other unknown reasons, the body attacks self- cells.
2. Rheumatoid arthritis is an example where body attacks self cells (synovial membrane).
3. So Statement I is correct but Statement II is incorrect.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Statement I correctly defines autoimmune disorder as immune attack on self cells.
2. Rheumatoid arthritis is a classic autoimmune disease where the body attacks its own joint tissues, so Statement II is false.

## Q159

*In-situ* conservation refers to:

- (2) Conserve only extinct species
- **(3)** Protect and conserve the whole ecosystem

## Q160

Biology · zoology · Biotechnology and Its Applications

In gene therapy of Adenosine Deaminase (ADA) deficiency, the patient requires periodic infusion of genetically engineered lymphocytes because :

- (1) Lymphocytes from patient's blood are grown in culture, outside the body.
- **(2)** Genetically engineered lymphocytes are not immortal cells.
- (3) Retroviral vector is introduced into these lymphocytes.
- (4) Gene isolated from marrow cells producing ADA is introduced into cells at embryonic stages

**Answer** — booklet: (2) · independent solve: (2) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Option (2) is the correct answer as genetically engineered lymphocyctes are not immortal cells and die after some time.
2. Option (1) is not the correct answer as the lymphocytes from patient's blood are grown in culture, outside the body but it is not the correct reason.
3. In option (4), if the gene isolated from bone marrow cells producing ADA is introduced into cells at early embryonic stages, it could be a permanent cure.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Engineered lymphocytes have a limited life span (they are not immortal), so the patient needs periodic infusions of them, which is option $2$.

**Subtopic:** Gene Therapy and Molecular Diagnosis · **Difficulty:** medium (2 steps)

## Q161

Biology · zoology · Structural Organisation in Animals

Which of the following is present between the adjacent bones of the vertebral column?

- (1) Areolar tissue
- (2) Smooth muscle
- (3) Intercalated discs
- **(4)** Cartilage

**Answer** — booklet: (4) · independent solve: (4) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Option (4) is the correct answer because cartilage forming the intervertebral disc is present between the adjacent bones of the vertebral column and it is a type of cartilaginous joint.
2. Option (1) is incorrect because areolar tissue present beneath the skin is a type of loose connective tissue.
3. Option (2) is incorrect because smooth muscles are present in the visceral organs.
4. Option (3) is incorrect because intercalated discs are characteristic feature of cardiac muscles present in heart.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Adjacent vertebrae are joined by intervertebral discs made of cartilage (fibrocartilage); intercalated discs belong to cardiac muscle, so the answer is option $4$.

**Subtopic:** Cartilage, Bone and Blood · **Difficulty:** easy (1 step)

## Q162

Biology · zoology · Digestion and Absorption

Which of the following functions is **not** performed by secretions from salivary glands?

- (1) Lubrication of oral cavity
- **(2)** Digestion of disaccharides
- (3) Control bacterial population in mouth
- (4) Digestion of complex carbohydrates

**Answer** — booklet: (2) · independent solve: (2) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Option (2) is the correct answer because digestion of polysaccharides like starch occurs in mouth and digestion of disaccharides occurs in small intestine.
2. Option (1) is incorrect because saliva contains mucus which helps in the lubrication of oral cavity.
3. Option (3) is incorrect because saliva contains an antibacterial agent-lysozyme so that it controls bacterial population in mouth.
4. Option (4) is incorrect because digestion of complex carbohydrates are performed by secretions from salivary glands.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Saliva contains lysozyme, which acts as an antibacterial agent and controls the bacterial population in the mouth, so option $3$ is performed.
2. Salivary amylase hydrolyses starch, a complex carbohydrate (polysaccharide), so option $4$ is performed.
3. Mucus in saliva lubricates the oral cavity and helps form the bolus, so option $1$ is performed.
4. Salivary amylase converts starch into the disaccharide maltose but does not break disaccharides down further; that happens in the intestine (maltase, sucrase, lactase), so option $2$ is not performed.

**Subtopic:** Digestion of Food · **Difficulty:** medium (2 steps)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 58)

![printed question 162](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/04112026/neet_papers/2022/2022_S1/crops/q162_18625dc1_04112026.png?v=18625dc1f970)

## Q163

Biology · zoology · Organisms and Populations

If '8' Drosophila in a laboratory population of '80' died during a week, the death rate in the population is ______ individuals per Drosophila per week.

- (1) $1.0$
- (2) zero
- **(3)** $0.1$
- (4) $10$

**Answer** — booklet: (3) · independent solve: (3) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. If $8$ *Drosophila* in a laboratory population of $80$ died during a week, the death rate in the population is $\frac{8}{80} = 0.1$ individuals per *Drosophila* per week.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Death rate = (Number of deaths)/(Initial population) = 8/80.
2. 8/80 = 0.1 individuals per Drosophila per week.

**Subtopic:** Life History Variation and Population Attributes · **Difficulty:** easy (1 step)

## Q164

Biology · zoology · Evolution

Natural selection where more individuals acquire specific character value other than the mean character value, leads to

- (1) Disruptive change
- (2) Random change
- (3) Stabilising change
- **(4)** Directional change

**Answer** — booklet: (4) · independent solve: (4) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Option (4) is correct because in directional natural selection more individuals acquire value other than the mean character value.
2. Option (1) is incorrect because in disruptive change, more individuals acquire peripheral character value at both ends of the distribution curve.
3. Option (2) is incorrect because there is no random change in natural selection.
4. Option (3) is incorrect because natural selection leads to stabilisation when more individuals acquire mean character value.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. As NCERT teaches it, in directional selection more individuals acquire a value other than the mean character value, so the peak shifts in one direction; this is option $4$ (Directional change).

**Subtopic:** Types of Natural Selection · **Difficulty:** medium (1 step)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 59)

## Q166

Biology · zoology · Structural Organisation in Animals

Which of the following is **not** a connective tissue?

- (1) Cartilage
- **(2)** Neuroglia
- (3) Blood
- (4) Adipose tissue

**Answer** — booklet: (2) · independent solve: (2) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Option (2) is the correct answer as neuroglia are a part of nervous tissue.
2. Neuroglia are the supportive cells of nervous tissue. They make up more than half the volume of neural tissue. Neurons, the unit of neural system are excitable cells.
3. Cartilage and blood are specialised type of connective tissues.
4. Adipose tissue is a type of loose connective tissue.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Blood (fluid), adipose tissue (loose) and cartilage (specialised) are all connective tissues, while neuroglia belong to neural tissue, so the answer is option $2$.

## Q167

Biology · zoology · Human Reproduction

At which stage of life the oogenesis process is initiated?

- (1) Birth
- (2) Adult
- (3) Puberty
- **(4)** Embryonic development stage

**Answer** — booklet: (4) · independent solve: (4) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Option (4) is the correct answer as oogenesis is initiated during the embryonic development stage when a couple of million gamete mother cells (oogonia) are formed within each foetal ovary.
2. No more oogonia are formed and added after birth in a human female.
3. At puberty only $60{,}000$ to $80{,}000$ primary follicles are left in each ovary, rest degenerate during the phase from birth to puberty.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Oogenesis starts in the embryonic stage: oogonia form in the foetal ovary and begin meiosis I, arresting as primary oocytes, so the answer is option $4$.

**Subtopic:** Female Gametogenesis (Oogenesis) · **Difficulty:** medium (1 step)

## Q169

Biology · zoology · Human Reproduction

Which of the following statements are true for spermatogenesis but do not hold true for Oogenesis?

Choose the most appropriate answer from the options given below:

- (a) It results in the formation of haploid gametes
- (b) Differentiation of gamete occurs after the completion of meiosis
- (c) Meiosis occurs continuously in a mitotically dividing stem cell population
- (d) It is controlled by the Luteinising hormone (LH) and Follicle Stimulating Hormone (FSH) secreted by the anterior pituitary
- (e) It is initiated at puberty

- (1) (b), (d) and (e) only
- **(2)** (b), (c) and (e) only
- (3) (c) and (e) only
- (4) (b) and (c) only

**Answer** — booklet: (2) · independent solve: (2) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Option (2) is the correct answer.
2. In both, spermatogenesis and oogenesis haploid gametes are formed. So (a) is true for both.
3. The spermatids are transformed into spermatozoa (sperms) by the process called spermiogenesis. Hence, (b) is true for spermatogenesis only.
4. Spermatogenesis and oogenesis both are controlled by LH and FSH secreted by the anterior pituitary. Hence (d) is true for both.
5. Spermatogenesis is a continuous process that begins at puberty. So (e) is true for spermatogenesis. Oogenesis on the other hand begins during embryonic development of the female.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. (a) Both spermatogenesis and oogenesis form haploid gametes, so (a) is not exclusive to spermatogenesis.
2. (b) Spermatids differentiate into sperms (spermiogenesis) after meiosis is complete; in oogenesis meiosis II finishes only after sperm entry, so (b) applies to spermatogenesis only.
3. (c) Spermatogonia form a mitotically dividing stem-cell population that keeps entering meiosis; oogonia stop multiplying before birth, so (c) applies to spermatogenesis only.
4. (d) Both processes are regulated by pituitary LH and FSH, so (d) is not exclusive.
5. (e) Spermatogenesis begins at puberty, whereas oogenesis begins during foetal development, so (e) applies to spermatogenesis only.
6. Hence (b), (c) and (e) only, which is option $2$.

**Subtopic:** Male Gametogenesis (Spermatogenesis), Female Gametogenesis (Oogenesis) · **Difficulty:** hard (5 steps)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 60)

![printed question 169](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/04112026/neet_papers/2022/2022_S1/crops/q169_135bfdd9_04112026.png?v=135bfdd9b693)

Publisher's solution, as printed:

![printed solution 169](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/04112026/neet_papers/2022/2022_S1/solutions/q169_8aa49ca2_04112026.png?v=8aa49ca246b8)

## Q172

Biology · zoology · The Living World

> ⚠ The printed solution says "None of the options are matching with the language of the question": the question has no correct option as printed, and the printed answer comes from a corrected reading.

> ⚠ The independent solve chose (unsure); the booklet prints (3).

In the taxonomic categories which hierarchical arrangement in ascending order is **correct** in case of animals?

- (1) Kingdom, Order, Class, Phylum, Family, Genus, Species
- (2) Kingdom, Order, Phylum, Class, Family, Genus, Species
- **(3)** Kingdom, Phylum, Class, Order, Family, Genus, Species
- (4) Kingdom, Class, Phylum, Family, Order, Genus, Species

**Answer** — booklet: (3) · independent solve: (unsure) · publisher_contradicts_itself

**Publisher's solution**

1. None of the options are matching with the language of the question
2. The correct ascending order of taxonomic categories in case of animals is
3. species $\rightarrow$ genus $\rightarrow$ family $\rightarrow$ order $\rightarrow$ class $\rightarrow$ phylum $\rightarrow$ kingdom

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. The animal hierarchy from lowest to highest rank is Species, Genus, Family, Order, Class, Phylum, Kingdom.
2. Ascending order therefore begins with Species and ends with Kingdom.
3. All four options begin with Kingdom, so all are written in descending direction; none is ascending.
4. Option 3 is the correct sequence only if read as descending.

**Subtopic:** Taxonomic Categories · **Difficulty:** easy (1 step)

## Q175

Biology · zoology · Digestion and Absorption

Given below are two statements :
Statement I :
Statement II :
In the light of the above statements, choose the most appropriate answer from the options given below:

- (Statement I) Fatty acids and glycerols cannot be absorbed into the blood.
- (Statement II) Specialized lymphatic capillaries called lacteals carry chylomicrons into lymphatic vessels and ultimately into the blood.

- (1) Statement I is correct but Statement II is incorrect
- (2) Statement I is incorrect but Statement II is correct
- **(3)** Both Statement I and Statement II are correct
- (4) Both Statement I and Statement II are incorrect

**Answer** — booklet: (3) · independent solve: (3) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Option (3) is the correct answer because both the statements I and II are correct as fatty acids and glycerol being insoluble in water, cannot be absorbed into the blood.
2. They are first incorporated into small droplets called micelles which move into the intestinal mucosa.
3. They are re-formed into very small protein coated fat globules called chylomicrons which are transported into the lymph vessels (lacteals) in the villi.
4. These lymph vessels ultimately release the absorbed substances into the blood stream.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. NCERT states fatty acids and glycerol, being insoluble, cannot be absorbed into the blood directly (Statement I correct); they are absorbed via micelles, re-formed into chylomicrons and transported by lacteals into lymphatic vessels and ultimately into the blood (Statement II correct), so option $3$.

**Subtopic:** Absorption of Digested Products · **Difficulty:** medium (3 steps)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 62)

## Q176

Biology · zoology · Animal Kingdom

Given below are two statements : one is labelled as Assertion (A) and the other is labelled as Reason (R).
In the light of the above statements, choose the **most appropriate** answer from the option given below :

- Assertion (A) All vertebrates are chordates but all chordates are not vertebrates.
- Reason (R) Notochord is replaced by vertebral column in the adult vertebrates.

- (1) (A) is correct but (R) is not correct
- (2) (A) is not correct but (R) is correct
- **(3)** Both (A) and (R) are correct and (R) is the correct explanation of (A)
- (4) Both (A) and (R) are correct but (R) is not the correct explanation of (A)

**Answer** — booklet: (3) · independent solve: (3) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Option (3) is the correct answer because all chordates are divided into three subphyla – Urochordata, Cephalochordata and Vertebrata.
2. In subphylum Vertebrata, notochord is replaced by bony or cartilaginous vertebral column in adults.
3. Therefore, all vertebrates are chordates but all chordates are not vertebrates.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Assertion: vertebrates are a subphylum of Chordata, while urochordates and cephalochordates are chordates without a vertebral column, so (A) is true.
2. Reason: in vertebrates the embryonic notochord is replaced by a cartilaginous or bony vertebral column in the adult, so (R) is true.
3. Having a vertebral column that replaces the notochord is what separates vertebrates from other chordates; NCERT gives (R) as the basis for (A), so (R) explains (A), which is option $3$.

**Subtopic:** Phylum – Chordata · **Difficulty:** hard (3 steps)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 62)

![printed question 176](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/04112026/neet_papers/2022/2022_S1/crops/q176_60334464_04112026.png?v=60334464c8ac)

## Q177

Biology · zoology · Cell Cycle and Cell Division

Regarding Meiosis, which of the statements is **incorrect**?

- (1) Pairing of homologous chromosomes and recombination occurs in Meiosis-I
- (2) Four haploid cells are formed at the end of Meiosis-II
- (3) There are two stages in Meiosis, Meiosis-I and II
- **(4)** DNA replication occurs in S phase of Meiosis-II

**Answer** — booklet: (4) · independent solve: (4) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Meiosis involves two sequential cycles of nuclear and cell division called meiosis-I and meiosis-II but only single cycle of DNA replication.
2. The stage between two meiotic divisions is called interkinesis and is generally short lived and involves no DNA replication.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Meiosis has two divisions: Meiosis-I (reductional) and Meiosis-II (equational), making option 3 correct.
2. Homologous chromosome pairing (synapsis) and crossing over/recombination occur during prophase of Meiosis-I, making option 1 correct.
3. At the end of Meiosis-II, four haploid cells are produced, making option 2 correct.
4. DNA replication occurs only once, during interphase before Meiosis-I begins; there is no S phase between Meiosis-I and Meiosis-II, so option 4 is incorrect.

**Subtopic:** Meiosis and Meiosis I · **Difficulty:** easy (1 step)

## Q178

Biology · zoology · Molecular Basis of Inheritance

If the length of a DNA molecule is $1.1$ metres, what will be the approximate number of base pairs?

- (1) $3.3 \times 10^{6}$ bp
- (2) $6.6 \times 10^{6}$ bp
- **(3)** $3.3 \times 10^{9}$ bp
- (4) $6.6 \times 10^{9}$ bp

**Answer** — booklet: (3) · independent solve: (3) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Number of base pairs $\times$ distance between 2 consecutive base pairs = Length of DNA molecule
2. $x \cdot 0.34 \times 10^{-9}\ \mathrm{m} = 1.1\ \mathrm{m}$
3. $x = \frac{1.1}{0.3 \times 10^{-9}}$
4. $= 3.6 \times 10^{9}$
5. $\simeq 3.3 \times 10^{9}$ bp

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Rise per base pair in B-DNA is $0.34\,nm = 3.4 \times 10^{-10}\,m$.
2. Number of base pairs $= \frac{1.1}{3.4 \times 10^{-10}} \approx 3.235 \times 10^{9}$.
3. This rounds to $3.3 \times 10^{9}$ bp, matching option 3.

**Subtopic:** Packaging of DNA Helix · **Difficulty:** medium (2 steps)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 63)

![printed question 178](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/04112026/neet_papers/2022/2022_S1/crops/q178_5b333009_04112026.png?v=5b3330090729)

## Q179

Biology · zoology · Cell Cycle and Cell Division

Select the **incorrect** statement with reference to mitosis:

- (1) Chromosomes decondense at telophase
- (2) Splitting of centromere occurs at anaphase
- (3) All the chromosomes lie at the equator at metaphase
- **(4)** Spindle fibres attach to centromere of chromosomes

**Answer** — booklet: (4) · independent solve: (4) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Spindle fibres attach to the kinetochores of chromosomes.
2. Kinetochores are the disc shaped structures present on sides of primary constriction or centromere of chromosomes.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Options 3, 1 and 2 match NCERT: chromosomes align at the equator in metaphase, the centromere splits at anaphase, and chromosomes decondense at telophase.
2. NCERT says spindle fibres attach to the kinetochores, small disc-shaped structures at the surface of the centromere, so option $4$ is the incorrect statement.

## Q180

Biology · zoology · Excretory Products and Their Elimination

Nitrogenous waste is excreted in the form of pellet or paste by :

- (1) Hippocampus
- **(2)** Pavo
- (3) Ornithorhynchus
- (4) Salamandra

**Answer** — booklet: (2) · independent solve: (2) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Option (2) is the correct answer because birds ($Pavo$) excrete nitrogenous wastes as uric acid in the form of pellet or paste with a minimum loss of water.
2. Option (1) and (4) are incorrect because many bony fishes (like $Hippocampus$) and aquatic amphibians (like $Salamandra$) are ammonotelic in nature.
3. Option (3) is incorrect because mammals (like $Ornithorhynchus$) mainly excrete urea and are called ureotelic animals.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Uricotelic animals (reptiles, birds, land snails, insects) excrete uric acid as a pellet or paste; Ornithorhynchus is a ureotelic mammal, Salamandra a ureotelic amphibian, Hippocampus an ammonotelic bony fish, and Pavo a uricotelic bird, so the answer is option $2$.

**Subtopic:** Excretory Waste and Structure of Excretory System in Animals · **Difficulty:** medium (2 steps)

## Q181

Biology · zoology · Animal Kingdom

In which of the following animals, digestive tract has additional chambers like crop and gizzard?

- (1) $\textit{Catla}$, $\textit{Columba}$, $\textit{Crocodilus}$
- **(2)** $\textit{Pavo}$, $\textit{Psittacula}$, $\textit{Corvus}$
- (3) $\textit{Corvus}$, $\textit{Columba}$, $\textit{Chameleon}$
- (4) $\textit{Bufo}$, $\textit{Balaenoptera}$, $\textit{Bangarus}$

**Answer** — booklet: (2) · independent solve: (2) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Option (2) is the correct answer because two additional chambers like crop and gizzard in alimentary canal are present in birds.
2. $\textit{Pavo}$ (Peacock), $\textit{Psittacula}$ (Parrot), $\textit{Corvus}$ (Crow) and $\textit{Columba}$ (Pigeon) are birds.
3. Option (1), (3) and (4) are incorrect because $\textit{Catla}$ is a bony fish, $\textit{Crocodilus}$, $\textit{Chameleon}$ and $\textit{Bangarus}$ are reptiles, $\textit{Bufo}$ is an amphibian and $\textit{Balaenoptera}$ is an aquatic mammal.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Crop (storage) and gizzard (grinding) are accessory chambers of the digestive tract typical of birds.
2. Check each option: Option 1 mixes fish, bird, reptile; Option 3 mixes birds with a reptile (Chameleon); Option 4 has amphibian, mammal, reptile - none are birds.
3. Option 2 (Pavo, Psittacula, Corvus) consists entirely of birds, all possessing crop and gizzard.

## Q182

Biology · zoology · Locomotion and Movement

Which of the following is a correct match for disease and its symptoms?

- (1) Myasthenia gravis – Genetic disorder resulting in weakening and paralysis of skeletal muscle
- (2) Muscular dystrophy – An auto immune disorder causing progressive degeneration of skeletal muscle
- **(3)** Arthritis – Inflammed joints
- (4) Tetany – High $\mathrm{Ca^{2+}}$ level causing rapid spasms.

**Answer** — booklet: (3) · independent solve: (3) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Option (3) is the correct answer because Arthritis is inflammation of joints.
2. Option (1) is incorrect because myasthenia gravis is an immune disorder affecting neuro-muscular junction leading to fatigue, weakening and paralysis of skeletal muscle.
3. Option (2) is incorrect because muscular dystrophy is progressive degeneration of skeletal muscle mostly due to genetic disorder.
4. Option (4) is incorrect because tetany is rapid spasms in muscle due to low $\mathrm{Ca^{++}}$ in body fluid.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Arthritis is inflammation of the joints, so option $3$ is a correct match.
2. Tetany is caused by low $\mathrm{Ca^{2+}}$ in body fluid, not high, so option $4$ is wrong.
3. Myasthenia gravis is an auto-immune disorder, not a genetic one, so option $1$ is wrong.
4. Muscular dystrophy is a genetic disorder, not auto-immune, so option $2$ is wrong; the answer is option $3$.

**Subtopic:** Disorders of Muscular and Skeletal System · **Difficulty:** hard (4 steps)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 64)

## Q183

Biology · zoology · Human Reproduction

Given below are two statements:

- (Statement I) The release of sperms into the seminiferous tubules is called spermiation.
- (Statement II) Spermiogenesis is the process of formation of sperms from spermatogonia.
- In the light of the above statements, choose the most appropriate answer from the options given below :

- **(1)** Statement I is correct but Statement II is incorrect
- (2) Statement I is incorrect but Statement II is correct
- (3) Both Statement I and Statement II are correct
- (4) Both Statement I and Statement II are incorrect

**Answer** — booklet: (1) · independent solve: (1) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Option (1) is the correct answer because Statement II is incorrect as the transformation of spermatids into spermatozoa (sperms) are called spermiogenesis. After this, sperm head becomes embedded in the Sertoli cells and are finally released from the seminiferous tubules by the process called spermiation.  Hence, Statement I is a correct statement.
2. Spermatogenesis is the process of formation of sperms from spermatogonia.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Statement I correctly describes spermiation as release of sperms into the lumen of seminiferous tubules.
2. Statement II is incorrect: spermiogenesis refers to transformation of spermatids into spermatozoa, not spermatogonia into sperms (that whole process is spermatogenesis).
3. Hence Statement I is correct but Statement II is incorrect, matching option 1.

**Subtopic:** Male Gametogenesis (Spermatogenesis) · **Difficulty:** hard (3 steps)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 64)

## Q184

Biology · zoology · Body Fluids and Circulation

Given below are two statements :
Statement I :
Statement II :
In the light of the above statements, choose the **most appropriate** answer from the options given below :

- (Statement I) The coagulum is formed of network of threads called thrombins.
- (Statement II) Spleen is the graveyard of erythrocytes.

- (1) Statement I is correct but Statement II is incorrect
- **(2)** Statement I is incorrect but Statement II is correct
- (3) Both Statement I and Statement II are correct
- (4) Both Statement I and Statement II are incorrect

**Answer** — booklet: (2) · independent solve: (2) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Option (2) is the correct answer because coagulum or clot is formed mainly of a network of threads called fibrins. Hence, Statement I is incorrect.
2. RBCs are destroyed in the spleen so spleen is known as the graveyard of erythrocytes. Hence, Statement II is correct.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Statement I: The network of threads forming the coagulum is composed of fibrin, not thrombin (thrombin is the enzyme that converts fibrinogen to fibrin) — this makes Statement I incorrect.
2. Statement II: NCERT textbook states that the spleen acts as a graveyard of erythrocytes (destroys worn-out RBCs) — this makes Statement II correct.
3. Hence, Statement I is incorrect but Statement II is correct, matching option 2.

**Subtopic:** Coagulation of Blood, Formed Elements And Their Types · **Difficulty:** medium (3 steps)

## Q186

Biology · zoology · Reproductive Health

Match **List-I** with **List-II** with respect to methods of Contraception and their respective actions.

Choose the **correct answer** from the options given below:

| List-I | | List-II | |
|---|---|---|---|
| (a) | Diaphragms | (i) | Inhibit ovulation and Implantation |
| (b) | Contraceptive Pills | (ii) | Increase phagocytosis of sperm within Uterus |
| (c) | Intra Uterine Devices | (iii) | Absence of Menstrual cycle and ovulation following parturition |
| (d) | Lactational Amenorrhea | (iv) | They cover the cervix blocking the entry of sperms |

- (1) (a) - (ii), (b) - (iv), (c) - (i), (d) - (iii)
- (2) (a) - (iii), (b) - (ii), (c) - (i), (d) - (iv)
- (3) (a) - (iv), (b) - (i), (c) - (iii), (d) - (ii)
- **(4)** (a) - (iv), (b) - (i), (c) - (ii), (d) - (iii)

**Answer** — booklet: (4) · independent solve: (4) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Option (4) is the correct answer because
2. Diaphragms are barrier methods of contraception. They cover the cervix and block the entry of sperms.
3. Contraceptive pills are preparations containing either progestogens alone or combination of progestogen and oestrogen. They inhibit ovulation and implantation as well as alter the quality of cervical mucus to prevent entry of sperms.
4. Intra uterine devices increase the phagocytosis of sperms within the uterus.
5. Lactational amenorrhoea is a natural method of contraception and it is based on the fact that the ovulation and therefore menstrual cycle do not occur during the period of intense lactation following parturition.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Diaphragms are barriers that cover the cervix and block sperm entry: $(a) \to (iv)$.
2. Contraceptive pills inhibit ovulation and implantation: $(b) \to (i)$.
3. Intra uterine devices increase phagocytosis of sperm within the uterus: $(c) \to (ii)$.
4. Lactational amenorrhea is the absence of menstruation and ovulation following parturition: $(d) \to (iii)$.
5. The match $a$-$iv$, $b$-$i$, $c$-$ii$, $d$-$iii$ is option $4$.

**Subtopic:** Artificial Contraceptive Methods, Natural Contraceptive Methods · **Difficulty:** easy (3 steps)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 66)

![printed question 186](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/04112026/neet_papers/2022/2022_S1/crops/q186_f052c70f_04112026.png?v=f052c70f60a9)

## Q188

Biology · zoology · Human Health and Disease

Select the **incorrect** statement with respect to acquired immunity.

- (1) Anamnestic response is due to memory of first encounter.
- **(2)** Acquired immunity is non-specific type of defense present at the time of birth.
- (3) Primary response is produced when our body encounters a pathogen for the first time.
- (4) Anamnestic response is elicited on subsequent encounters with the same pathogen.

**Answer** — booklet: (2) · independent solve: (2) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Option (2) is the correct answer as acquired immunity is a specific type of defence which is not present at the time of birth.
2. Option (1), (3) and (4) are true statements and hence cannot be the answer.
3. Anamnestic response or secondary immune response is a highly intensified response due to memory of first encounter.
4. When our body encounters a pathogen for the first time then the body elicits the primary immune response.
5. When there is a subsequent encounter with the same pathogen, secondary or anamnestic immune response is elicited.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. Acquired immunity is pathogen-specific, has memory and is not present at birth; innate immunity is the non-specific defence present at birth, so statement $4$ is the incorrect one.

**Subtopic:** Acquired Immunity · **Difficulty:** easy (1 step)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 67)

## Q189

Biology · zoology · Chemical Coordination and Integration

Which of the following are **not** the effects of Parathyroid hormone?

- (a) Stimulates the process of bone resorption
- (b) Decreases $\mathrm{Ca^{2+}}$ level in blood
- (c) Reabsorption of $\mathrm{Ca^{2+}}$ by renal tubules
- (d) Decreases the absorption of $\mathrm{Ca^{2+}}$ from digested food
- (e) Increases metabolism of carbohydrates

- (1) (a) and (e) only
- (2) (b) and (c) only
- (3) (a) and (c) only
- **(4)** (b), (d) and (e) only

**Answer** — booklet: (4) · independent solve: (4) · agree

**Publisher's solution**

1. Option (4) is the correct answer because parathyroid hormone is a hypercalcemic hormone $i.e$, it increases the blood calcium levels. It also increases the absorption of calcium from digested food. Glucocorticoids regulate the carbohydrate metabolism.
2. Option (1) is not the answer because parathyroid hormone stimulates the process of bone resorption.
3. Option (2) and (3) are not the answers because reabsorption of $\mathrm{Ca^{2+}}$ by renal tubules is a function of PTH.

**Solved independently** (answer withheld)

1. PTH raises blood Ca2+ via bone resorption (a is a real effect, so excluded from 'not' list).
2. PTH promotes renal tubular reabsorption of Ca2+ (c is a real effect, excluded).
3. PTH increases, not decreases, blood Ca2+ level, so (b) is NOT an effect as stated.
4. PTH increases, not decreases, intestinal absorption of Ca2+ (via vitamin D activation), so (d) is NOT an effect as stated.
5. Carbohydrate metabolism regulation is not a function of PTH, so (e) is NOT an effect.
6. Thus (b), (d), (e) are not effects of PTH, matching option 4.

**Subtopic:** Thyroid Gland and Parathyroid Gland · **Difficulty:** hard (4 steps)

**Printed in the booklet** (page 67)

![printed question 189](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/04112026/neet_papers/2022/2022_S1/crops/q189_2e7e435f_04112026.png?v=2e7e435fdb42)

Publisher's solution, as printed:

![printed solution 189](https://d30lmobgrc5gb8.cloudfront.net/general/04112026/neet_papers/2022/2022_S1/solutions/q189_b02652b8_04112026.png?v=b02652b85b14)
